# a12-dmkits specification synchronization ledger

This ledger records outbound reconciliation of changes to the project-owned language-neutral semantics under [`../spec/`](../spec/). Pending entries form the outbound queue. Those files are the semantic bridge between a12-kernel-lean and a12-dmkits: locally originated corrections and additions canonized in `spec/` must be reconciled back into a12-dmkits, while findings already committed and reviewed in a12-dmkits are incorporated locally as inbound provenance without entering this queue. This ledger tracks outbound reconciliation without becoming a second semantic specification.

## Ledger contract

Every commit that corrects, narrows, or extends kernel behavior in `spec/` and still needs a12-dmkits reconciliation must add or update a ledger entry here in the same change. The spec clause owns the complete semantic account; an entry links to that clause and records only the transport facts needed by the other project.

An inbound correction whose exact source revision is already committed and reviewed in a12-dmkits does not need reconciliation back to its origin and therefore creates no new ledger entry. Record that revision and its evidence routes in [`SOURCES.md`](SOURCES.md), plus any local implementation boundary in its owning document. If the inbound result answers an existing `pending` or `handed-off` entry, update that same entry instead. Never create a new pending entry merely because an inbound handoff template requests one for findings that the cited a12-dmkits revision already owns.

Pure spelling, formatting, link, or non-semantic navigation edits do not enter the ledger. One entry may group several clauses only when they express one coherent behavioral correction with one upstream acceptance decision. Keep entries concise: do not copy the full spec text, Lean implementation status, or evidence inventory into this ledger.

Each entry has a stable `SPEC-YYYY-MM-DD-NN` ID and records:

- status: `pending`, `handed-off`, `accepted`, `resolved`, or `superseded`. `accepted` means this repository reviewed an exact a12-dmkits revision against the entry's acceptance condition. `resolved` is the terminal state for an `EXP-` entry closed **locally** as requiring no observation — no upstream revision exists to review, so `accepted` would misdescribe it. An `EXP-` entry answered by an upstream probe uses `accepted` as usual;
- the changed spec file and section;
- a concise delta summary;
- the kernel version and evidence or source basis;
- the exact a12-dmkits revision whose source, prose, and tests were audited as the entry's basis;
- the expected a12-dmkits reconciliation surfaces;
- compatibility and testing consequences;
- the local introducing revision and, after acceptance, the reviewed a12-dmkits revision and disposition;
- a `Supersedes` or `Superseded by` link when applicable.

Status advances as follows:

1. `pending` — committed here but not yet delivered to the a12-dmkits maintainer or agent;
2. `handed-off` — delivered with the exact entry IDs and local revision; a returned handback remains here until reviewed;
3. `accepted` — this repository reviewed the exact a12-dmkits revision against the entry's acceptance condition and relevant gates, then recorded the result;
4. `resolved` — an `EXP-` entry closed locally as requiring no observation, per the definition above; no upstream revision exists to review;
5. `superseded` — a successor replaced a pending or handed-off entry and names it explicitly.

A returned handback that is neither an observation nor a reasoned refusal leaves the entry `handed-off` with its inconclusive outcome recorded. Do not promote it to `accepted`: the acceptance condition is unmet, and recording the mechanism learned along the way is not the same as answering the question.

Stable IDs are never reused. An accepted entry is an immutable reconciliation receipt; a later correction creates a new entry with `Supersedes`, while the accepted predecessor remains accepted. Compact accepted and superseded entries stay in this ledger rather than being deleted or copied into an archive.

The user transfers pending entries. Treat the entire `../a12-rulekit/` checkout as read-only at all times: never edit, generate, clean, stage, commit, or create visible files there. Reconciliation from this repository means inspecting an exact committed a12-dmkits revision and recording the reviewed outcome in this ledger; concurrent sibling changes are only observed and left untouched. When a handback arrives, update the same entry with its outcome; do not create a second feedback ledger. A no-code disposition is valid when a12-dmkits already implements and tests the behavior but needs only documentation reconciliation or an explicit acknowledgement.

If a12-dmkits returns contrary evidence, do not mark the entry accepted. Verify the disagreement against the real kernel. An inconclusive handback remains `handed-off`; a confirmed local error is corrected in `spec/` by a successor entry that supersedes the old pending/handed-off entry, while a confirmed upstream error is returned for correction under the same entry.

The introducing revision cannot name itself inside the same commit. Until handoff, `introducing commit` means the first commit containing the stable entry ID; resolve it with:

```sh
git log -S 'SPEC-YYYY-MM-DD-NN' --format='%H' -- docs/A12-DMKITS-SPEC-SYNC-LEDGER.md
```

## Kinds of entry

The ledger carries two kinds, distinguished by identifier prefix, because they ask for different things and must not be confused.

- **`SPEC-…` — a semantic correction.** A behavioral `spec/` change originated here that still needs a12-dmkits reconciliation. This is the ledger's original and dominant purpose; the rules above govern it.
- **`EXP-…` — an experiment request.** A request to *run a specific probe*, because this project has identified a case whose outcome it cannot determine and which no existing evidence route exercises. It changes no `spec/` clause and asserts no correction; a disposition may legitimately be "observed, and the local account was already right."

The distinction matters because the two arms of this project's convergence design fail differently. A correction transports knowledge the analytical arm has; an experiment request supplies a *shape* the empirical arm cannot generate for itself. The second is the mechanism for shared blind spots — cases where both projects agree because neither has probed, rather than because the account is right. An `EXP-` entry must therefore name the exact input shape, the competing accounts, what each predicts, and why existing coverage cannot reach it.

It must also **verify that the named discriminator is the actual variable**, because competing accounts can both be true on loci the entry never distinguished. [`EXP-2026-07-25-03`](#exp-2026-07-25-03--the-semantic-index-computation-gate-may-be-route-selected-not-read-time) posed a route-selected mechanism against a uniform one and both turned out to hold — separated by which index group carries the malformed key, a variable the entry never mentioned. Such a request cannot be answered as asked: any observation confirms one account without refuting the other, and the requested single document cannot separate them. Before handing off, state what a *negative* result would look like; if no input distinguishes the accounts, the discriminator is wrong and the entry is not yet an experiment.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-08 — `Time(...)` component fields use exact positional declaration gates

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic extension
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `024b27550364e9e317315aad58bd272a63d5c587`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §3](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#3-constructing-dates-and-checking-validity)
- **Delta:** A Number field used as a `Time(...)` component must have zero minimum and maximum fractional digits, a nonnegative domain, and either maximum stored length 2 or the exact maximum for its position; `maxIntegerDigits = 2` is not equivalent. A String field must have maximum length 2 and either be an extensible enumeration or carry one of the checker's six recognized digit-only patterns. Extractor components must match Hour, Minute, or Second position exactly.
- **Basis:** [`CheckTimeKonstruktImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckTimeKonstruktImpl.java) supplies the positional maxima and exact extractor token. [`CheckBaseDateConstructionImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckBaseDateConstructionImpl.java) checks a Number field's `getMaxLength`, not its integer-digit cap, and owns the fractional, nonnegative-domain, String-length, extensible-enumeration, and recognized-pattern alternatives. [`CodeGenCreator.createDatumParameter`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/conversion/CodeGenCreator.java) changes a field component to Number operation type, and the generated Java/Groovy/JavaScript `FieldIdOperation.st` routes therefore call `feldWertAlsZahl`; the Java [`RuntimeController`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) and TypeScript [`BigDecimalUtils`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime-ts/src/main/js/internal/core/BigDecimalUtils.ts) implementations parse an admitted nonempty digit String as decimal and preserve empty input as zero with symmetric missing provenance. The Java/Groovy/JavaScript [`TimeConstructionOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/TimeConstructionOperation.st) variants preserve Hour/Minute/Second evaluation order. At the inspected a12-dmkits basis revision, `ConstructedDateCalendarDiffTest` covers a full three-Number-field construction, while the shared evaluator requires exactly three arguments and no maintained static matrix separates the declaration gates.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Extend the existing Time-construction checker and evaluator owners, not the harness. Preserve the existing full-field differential and add static acceptance/rejection controls that separate maximum stored length from `maxIntegerDigits`, exact 23 versus 59 position bounds, fractional and signed-without-nonnegative-minimum Number declarations, the extensible-enumeration String arm, every recognized digit-only String pattern, wrong extractors, and one-to-three field prefixes. Add runtime String controls for leading-zero decimal conversion, empty-input incompleteness, and a two-digit but impossible hour remaining present/unreal.
- **Compatibility:** Treating any two-integer-digit Number as fixed-width admits declarations the Kernel rejects. Requiring both length and exact maximum, accepting a merely smaller maximum, rejecting the extensible-enumeration String arm, or accepting arbitrary digit-shaped regexes changes static legality. A generic extractor erases the positional mismatch error.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical prose and the peer checker/evaluator agree on every named declaration branch and prefix arity; maintained JVM/Node controls and both available kernel strategies retain the full three-field path plus the separating static cases; the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-09 — `Time(...)` extractor components admit nonliteral Time-bearing sources

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic extension
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `422dda2626cea8689935c87ea380a17bcd15cdfe`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §3](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#3-constructing-dates-and-checking-validity)
- **Delta:** An extractor component must use the matching `HoursFromTime`, `MinutesFromTime`, or `SecondsFromTime` token, but its operand is any ordinary nonliteral Time- or DateTime-bearing expression whose result format exposes that component, not only a direct field. Direct temporal literals inside the extractor are rejected. Number, String, and extractor forms may be mixed across one `Time(...)` prefix. An empty extractor source contributes numeric zero with missing provenance, so the enclosing constructor is incomplete rather than midnight.
- **Basis:** [`timeKonstrukt` and `datumAngabe`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/antlr4/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/grammar/gen/GrammarOnly_ConditionParser.g4) place a complete `datumExtractOp` in any supplied component and give the extractor its recursive ordinary temporal operand. [`CheckTimeKonstruktImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckTimeKonstruktImpl.java) checks the exact positional token, while [`CheckDatumExtractOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumExtractOpImpl.java) rejects only direct temporal constants before checking the complete source result format. [`DateExtractionOperation`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/fieldoperations/date/DateExtractionOperation.java) retains a nested `CodeGenOperation`; every Java/Groovy/JavaScript [`DateExtractionOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/DateExtractionOperation.st) evaluates that source before the extractor call; and [`BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDatumsTeil`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/BedingungsOperatorHelper.java) supplies symmetric missing zero. At the inspected a12-dmkits basis revision, direct Time/DateTime component extraction exists, but `evaluateTimeConstruction` still requires exactly three generic expressions and no maintained case separates a mixed prefix, selected nested extractor source, or the direct-literal rejection.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Extend the existing Time-construction and temporal-expression owners, not the harness. Preserve the current direct extractor behavior and add the smallest static/runtime cases for a mixed Number/extractor prefix, Time and DateTime field sources, an empty extractor source that leaves the constructor incomplete, one wider admitted source such as `HoursFromTime(AddHours(...))`, and the rejected direct-literal and wrong-position controls.
- **Compatibility:** Restricting extractor sources to fields rejects legal nested temporal expressions. Accepting a direct literal inside an extractor or a mismatched token widens static legality. Dropping the extractor's missing provenance incorrectly turns an empty source into a regular zero and can manufacture midnight.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical prose, typed authoring, conversion, and evaluation retain the source expression and exact positional token; maintained JVM/Node controls and both available kernel strategies separate all requested accounts; existing direct temporal-component and Time-construction owners remain singular; the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-07 — `TimeFromDateTime` projects the model-zone wall clock

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic extension
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `38ee82962c3edb6ef8db493d32665e270de2e778`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §3](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#3-constructing-dates-and-checking-validity)
- **Delta:** `TimeFromDateTime` statically requires a complete DateTime operand. It preserves a valueless source's reason; for a present source it reads the model-zone Hour/Minute/Second and returns a 1970-01-01 Time carrier, so downstream Time consumers observe only that wall clock. An enclosing `DateTime(date, TimeFromDateTime(source))` still evaluates Date first.
- **Basis:** [`CheckDatumExtractOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumExtractOpImpl.java) admits the operation only when all six DateTime components are present and assigns the Time result format. [`DateExtractionOperation`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/fieldoperations/date/DateExtractionOperation.java) plus the Java/Groovy/JavaScript [`DateExtractionOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/DateExtractionOperation.st) variants generate one direct runtime call after evaluating the source. [`RuntimeController.getZeitAusDateTime`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) returns a valueless `VkDate` unchanged; otherwise it reads model-zone clock fields and delegates the 1970 anchor to [`DateUtil.createTime`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/DateUtil.java). The maintained a12-dmkits `DateTimeExtractDiffTest` and `DstTimeZoneDiffTest` already lock direct complete-DateTime extraction and overlap-hour wall components, while its typed evaluator admits wider DateTime expressions; no maintained case composes the extraction with the partial-Date `ValueAsDate` boundary.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Extend the existing temporal extraction and DateTime-construction owners, not the harness. State the complete-DateTime gate, valueless-reason preservation, wall-clock-only result, and outer Date-before-Time order. Retain the existing ordinary and overlap extraction differentials, add static rejection of one missing component, and add compact JVM/Node plus both-kernel-route controls for `DateTime(ValueAsDate(partial, LastDay), TimeFromDateTime(source))`, empty source, and Date-side formal short-circuit.
- **Compatibility:** Accepting Date, Time, or partial DateTime sources changes static legality. Reusing the source exact instant makes a Time-only consumer depend on the discarded Date and DST occurrence. Re-resolving a retained ambiguous label may choose another instant, while evaluating the extraction before the outer Date changes which formal cause wins.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical prose and the typed evaluator agree on admission, reason propagation, wall-clock projection, and outer order; maintained JVM/Node and both available kernel routes retain the ordinary, overlap, empty, static-rejection, and partial-Date composition separators; the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-06 — Time constants are exact locale-independent `HH:mm:ss`

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic extension
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `572aadf732733a47c1433c2ce699333b3d33e7de`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §1](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#1-constant-format-and-the-stringdate-literal-ambiguity-)
- **Delta:** A quoted Time constant is classified statically by exact ASCII `HH:mm:ss` syntax and real-clock bounds, independently of locale and operand context. Invalid width, punctuation, digit repertoire, or range is an invalid date string and never a runtime String/Time value. Runtime construction uses a 1970-01-01 model-zone carrier, while Time consumers observe the decoded clock components.
- **Basis:** [`datumAngabe`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/antlr4/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/grammar/gen/GrammarOnly_ConditionParser.g4) admits a `StringLiteral` in every date/time operand position. [`CheckDatumAngabeImpl.checkDatumString`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumAngabeImpl.java) delegates to [`DateUtils`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/DateUtils.java), whose eight-character ASCII pattern, fixed `HH:mm:ss` format, and non-lenient parse jointly enforce exact widths and 00–23/00–59/00–59 reality. [`ConstantOperation`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/fieldoperations/ConstantOperation.java) retains the inferred Date operation type and format; Java/Groovy/JavaScript [`ConstantOperation_datum.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/ConstantOperation_datum.st) pass both unchanged to [`RuntimeController.constructDatum`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java), which uses the model-zone parser. a12-dmkits already exposes `TimeLiteral`/`Dsl.timeValue` and renders exact quoted text, but no inspected maintained differential separates a literal in the partial-Date DateTime position.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Extend the existing Time-literal owner and tests, not the harness: state the exact ASCII fixed-width and real-clock admission in the canonical semantics; reject wrong width, dot punctuation, non-ASCII digits, `24:00:00`, and `23:59:60`; and retain one kernel-backed `DateTime(ValueAsDate(...), "10:30:45")` acceptance case under the model zone.
- **Compatibility:** Contextual String coercion would admit text the Kernel rejects or mis-type a valid Time constant. Locale parsing, relaxed widths, or leap-second acceptance changes static legality. Retaining a model-zone instant instead of the decoded clock can make a Time-only consumer depend spuriously on the hidden 1970 carrier.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical prose and the checked decoder agree on exact syntax and bounds; maintained JVM/Node and both-kernel-route cases retain at least one accepted literal, every named rejection class, and the partial-Date DateTime composition; the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-05 — `Time(...)` defaults trailing components and retains no-value reasons

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic extension
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `52b10661f2d4ce21e0809442ce0c8867cf4bad7a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §3](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#3-constructing-dates-and-checking-validity)
- **Delta:** `Time(...)` accepts a leading prefix of zero through three checked components and supplies fixed zeroes for every omitted trailing component. Formal/non-relevant unavailability dominates missingness; a missing supplied component is incomplete/not-given, while a fully present impossible clock is a distinct present/unreal no-value. `DateTime(date, time)` evaluates Date first and preserves that Time-side distinction.
- **Basis:** [`timeKonstrukt`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/antlr4/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/grammar/gen/GrammarOnly_ConditionParser.g4) fixes the four prefix shapes. [`CheckTimeKonstruktImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckTimeKonstruktImpl.java) restricts constants, field declarations, and extractor positions; [`TimeConstructionOperation`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/fieldoperations/date/TimeConstructionOperation.java) supplies numeric zeroes for omitted slots; generated [`TimeConstructionOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/TimeConstructionOperation.st) preserves hour/minute/second argument order; and [`RuntimeController.constructTime`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) plus [`DateUtil.createTime`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/DateUtil.java) establish non-relevant/missing/reality precedence and the 1970 model-zone anchor. The peer's maintained `DateConstructionDiffTest` locks only a full valid literal; its evaluator currently assigns not-given to an out-of-range fully present clock and its public DSL exposes only the three-argument form.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Extend the existing Time-construction owner, not the harness: admit and render zero-, one-, and two-component prefixes with trailing zero defaults; retain incomplete versus present/unreal no-value; and preserve Date-before-Time evaluation in DateTime composition. Add compact JVM/Node and both-kernel-route separators for `Time()`, `Time("10")`, `Time("10","30")`, one empty checked field component, one fully present impossible runtime clock if reachable through the maintained fixture, and one Date-side formal failure that prevents the Time operand from deciding the result.
- **Compatibility:** Requiring exactly three arguments rejects legal syntax. Treating omitted slots as missing makes `Time()` valueless instead of midnight. Collapsing a fully present impossible clock into not-given changes downstream reason and polarity behavior; evaluating Time first changes which formal failure wins.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical prose and the shared evaluator state all four arities and both no-value reasons; maintained authoring/runtime cases cover the separators above on JVM, Node, and both available kernel strategies or document why the impossible-clock arm is statically unreachable; the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-04 — Date-shift amounts use Java signed-32-bit narrowing

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `d4fab2d2fe2f4d02ef79499e81f804daacd4ac51`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §2](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#2-arithmetic-and-difference)
- **Delta:** Every Date/DateTime `Add*` amount is an ordinary numeric operation and is converted through Java `BigDecimal.intValue()`: truncate the decimal toward zero, then retain the low signed 32 bits. Large amounts are neither rejected nor saturated. Numeric-expression evaluation preserves its ordinary directional missing provenance, so an empty direct Number field supplies zero and an expression such as that field plus `1` supplies concrete `1`, both marked not-given. A domain-invalid amount such as division by zero supplies no amount and makes the Date/DateTime result valueless. The generated operation evaluates the source before the amount: a formal source stops first, while a cause-free empty source still reaches the complete amount expression.
- **Basis:** Grammar [`datumAddOp`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/antlr4/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/grammar/gen/GrammarOnly_ConditionParser.g4) retains the amount as a complete `operation`, and [`CheckDatumAddOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumAddOpImpl.java) requires its result type to be Number rather than narrowing the syntax to a field or literal. Generated [`DateAddOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/DateAddOperation.st) evaluates the Date operand before the generated numeric operation and passes both to the runtime controller. Generated [`PointInTimeOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/PointInTimeOperation.st) supplies dynamic `Now` through the same nested source position, and generated [`FieldIdOperation.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/FieldIdOperation.st) routes a Number field through [`RuntimeController.feldWertAlsZahl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java), whose empty branch constructs numeric zero with directional fillability. [`BedingungsOperatorHelper.addTime`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/BedingungsOperatorHelper.java) derives Date not-given from the source or either amount-fillability direction, maps the invalid-number sentinel to a valueless Date, and calls [`VkBigDecimal.intValue`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/VkBigDecimal.java), which delegates directly to `java.math.BigDecimal.intValue()`. [`RuntimeController.getZeitAusDateTime`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) and `constructDateTime` retain missing provenance on the concrete extracted clock and reconstructed DateTime. The reviewed a12-dmkits evaluator at the basis revision converts its decimal amount with Kotlin `toInt()` and has no maintained overflow, missing-expression, or domain-invalid Date-amount separator, so equivalence at those boundaries is not established.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** State the signed-32-bit narrowing, numeric-expression fillability/domain-failure projection, and source-before-amount rules in the canonical Date arithmetic account; verify the multiplatform decimal conversion against Java `BigDecimal.intValue()` and correct it if necessary. Add focused JVM/Node and both-kernel-route controls for `1.9 → 1`, `-1.9 → -1`, `2147483648 → -2147483648`, and `-2147483649 → 2147483647`, plus one valid DateTime source shifted by an empty direct Number field and consumed through `DateTime(ValueAsDate(...), TimeFromDateTime(...))`. Add expression separators for empty field plus `1` retaining a concrete omission-typed shift and field divided by zero yielding a valueless DateTime, with a `Now`-source companion if its maintained lowering route is separately owned. If the maintained fixture exposes formal reads, also separate a formal source from a formal amount expression and an empty source followed by that formal amount. Reuse the existing Date arithmetic owner and harness.
- **Compatibility:** Rounding changes ordinary fractional shifts. Saturation, range rejection, arbitrary-precision offsets, or a platform-specific decimal-to-int conversion changes reachable results at and beyond the signed-32-bit boundary. Treating an empty amount as no value erases a concrete result; treating it as fixed zero changes downstream polarity. Treating a domain-invalid expression as numeric zero manufactures a shifted value. Evaluating the amount before the source changes the first reached formal cause.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical prose names truncation plus low signed-32-bit narrowing, numeric-expression fillability and domain failure, and source-before-amount order; the shared evaluator has one platform-independent conversion with the four numeric separators, retains concrete omission-typed direct and expression shifts, and keeps a domain-invalid amount valueless; maintained JVM/Node and both available kernel strategies agree, and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-03 — partial-Date precision forms a suffix and unknown year is non-relevant

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic extension
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `e3d6415e894365221c0b37c2880b5e215eba4946`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §6](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#6-date-fragments-and-fragment-ranges)
- **Delta:** `MONTH_OPTIONAL` and `YEAR_OPTIONAL` extend the existing `DAY_OPTIONAL` stored-value domain monotonically. Unknown components must be a suffix: an unknown month requires an unknown day, and an unknown year requires both month and day unknown. `ValueAsDate` completes an unknown month to January/December before the day boundary, but returns a non-relevant Date immediately when the year is unknown; direct comparison therefore evaluates to UNKNOWN, not ordinary non-firing emptiness, and the year is not replaced with 2000 for operation evaluation.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`IDateType.DatePrecision`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-model-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/model/api/fieldtypes/IDateType.java) specifies the legal omission shapes. [`TeilbekanntesDatumUtil`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/TeilbekanntesDatumUtil.java) enforces exact `00`/`0000` markers and suffix consistency, while [`FormatDefinitionDatum`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/formatdef/FormatDefinitionDatum.java) uses `01.01.2000` only for formal validation. [`RuntimeController.wertAlsDatum`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) checks unknown year first and returns `VkDate.NON_RELEVANT_DATE`; [`RuntimeController.vergleicheDATUM`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) then maps any non-relevant operand to `FALSE_OR_UNKNOWN`. Otherwise [`PartiallyKnownDatesUtil`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/PartiallyKnownDatesUtil.java) completes month to `01`/`12` before resolving day to `01`/month end. The maintained peer loader explicitly rejects `MONTH_OPTIONAL` and `YEAR_OPTIONAL`, so its day-optional differential does not cover this extension.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Extend the existing `DatePrecision`, formal-check, raw-value, and `ValueAsDate` owners to `MONTH_OPTIONAL` and `YEAR_OPTIONAL`; do not create a second partial-Date representation. Preserve unknown components until the operation selects a boundary, and keep the unknown-year non-relevant outcome distinct from empty, malformed, or a synthetic year. Add focused maintained JVM/Node and both-kernel-route cases rather than a new harness.
- **Compatibility:** Treating each enum member as permitting only its named omission rejects legal less-partial values. Completing unknown year with the formal checker's temporary 2000 can make an enclosing comparison spuriously fire; collapsing it to ordinary emptiness loses the kernel's UNKNOWN result. Permitting `DD.00.YYYY` or `DD.MM.0000` accepts values the kernel rejects formally.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical prose and model/runtime APIs name the complete suffix matrix; maintained cases separate month-first from month-last, known-year day omission under the wider modes, unknown-year UNKNOWN from both ordinary empty non-firing and year-2000 completion, and both illegal mixed omission shapes across JVM, Node, and both kernel strategies; the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-02 — `ValueAsDate` is admitted in explicit nested Date positions

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `e3d6415e894365221c0b37c2880b5e215eba4946`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`05-dates-and-time.md` §6](../spec/05-dates-and-time.md#6-date-fragments-and-fragment-ranges)
- **Delta:** `ValueAsDate(field, FirstDay|LastDay)` is not direct-comparison-only. It is a Date-valued operation, and the grammar's special `datumAngabe2` operand explicitly admits it in Date addition, Date difference, and the Date argument of `DateTime(...)`; ordinary Date extraction keeps the narrower `datumAngabe` operand and rejects the nested form.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`GrammarOnly_ConditionParser.g4`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/antlr4/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/grammar/gen/GrammarOnly_ConditionParser.g4) defines `datumAngabe2` as an ordinary Date operand or `feldOpMitDatumSpecial` and uses it in `datumAddOp`, `datumDiffOp`, and `dateTimeKonstrukt`, while `datumExtractOp` uses only `datumAngabe`. [`CheckDatumAddOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumAddOpImpl.java), [`CheckDatumDiffOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumDiffOpImpl.java), and [`CheckDateTimeKonstruktImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDateTimeKonstruktImpl.java) deliberately bypass ordinary partial-Date rejection for this special operand. Generated Java, Groovy, and JavaScript [`FieldIdOperationWithParameters.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-condition/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/operationsDir/FieldIdOperationWithParameters.st) variants preserve the selected endpoint in the runtime call. The maintained peer `ValueAsDateDiffTest` locks direct comparison only, and its extractor rejection does not separate the newly corrected nested positions.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Correct the canonical Date account and any finding that infers direct-only placement from the extractor rejection. Reuse the existing `ValueAsDate` operation and Date add/difference/DateTime owners; add the smallest maintained authoring/runtime controls for one nested Date addition, one difference or `DateTime(...)` composition, and the rejected ordinary extractor control. Do not widen the operation to arbitrary expression positions, create a second partial-Date value, or add a new harness.
- **Compatibility:** A direct-only checker rejects kernel-legal models before evaluation. Generalizing from the explicit `datumAngabe2` positions to every Date consumer is also wrong: ordinary extraction uses a narrower grammar and remains rejected.
- **Acceptance:** Peer prose names the explicit nested positions and the extractor exclusion; maintained JVM, Node, and both available kernel strategies separate at least one admitted nested position from the rejected extractor; existing operation and Date consumer owners remain singular; and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision plus per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-27-01 — computed Dates remain concrete in partial-precision targets

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `e3d6415e894365221c0b37c2880b5e215eba4946`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`09-computations.md` §4](../spec/09-computations.md#4-the-stored-form--a-computed-value-lands-as-a-string-in-the-targets-shape)
- **Delta:** A Date computation produces a concrete `VkDate`, renders that instant in the target's exact declared format and model zone, and only then runs the target's reduced basic check. Every Date precision permits a fully known value, so `DAY_OPTIONAL`, `MONTH_OPTIONAL`, and `YEAR_OPTIONAL` targets store the same fully known computed text as `FULL`; computation never manufactures `00`/`0000` fragments. Partial-fragment consistency accepts that attempt before the ordinary additional-date and lower-bound checks.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`IDateType.DatePrecision`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-model-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/model/api/fieldtypes/IDateType.java) explicitly admits fully known dates in all four modes. Generated [`CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st) passes the selected result to [`CalculationController.handleBerechnetenWert(VkDate, ...)`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/CalculationController.java), which renders through [`ValidationDateParser.makeStringDayMonthTwoDigits`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/ValidationDateParser.java) before `checkField`. [`FormatDefinitionDatum.validiereFormat`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/formatdef/FormatDefinitionDatum.java) runs partial-fragment consistency first, and [`TeilbekanntesDatumUtil`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/TeilbekanntesDatumUtil.java) accepts fully known fragments for every mode.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Add this rule to the canonical computation/Date account and reuse the existing Date target renderer and basic-check owner. Admit computed Date targets for all four Date precisions instead of either rejecting partial modes or converting a concrete result to an unknown-fragment value. Extend the existing computation/date target family with the same instant assigned to `FULL`, `DAY_OPTIONAL`, `MONTH_OPTIONAL`, and `YEAR_OPTIONAL` targets, plus a pre-1900 enabled control proving the additional check still rejects the exact rendered attempt. The current interpreter supports only `DAY_OPTIONAL`; reconcile the two wider modes at their existing model/loader/formal owner rather than adding a computation-only precision type.
- **Compatibility:** Rejecting a partial-precision target blocks a kernel-legal computation. Replacing concrete fragments with zeroes stores a different value and changes downstream Date semantics. Skipping the later basic checks accepts a pre-1900 attempt that the kernel reports as an errored computed value.
- **Acceptance:** Peer prose states the render-first concrete-result rule; maintained JVM, Node, and both available kernel-strategy cases cover all four precision modes plus the pre-1900 separator; the existing Date precision, renderer, formal-check, and computation owners remain singular; and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision plus per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-26-01 — starred group-list iteration guards are operator- and operand-sensitive

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `45d24b73549124bf7da342a1ee19401408438fc6`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §1](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#1-when-a-rule-iterates-and-where-its-error-lands)
- **Delta:** A starred group-list operand contributes only its captured prefix to ordinary rule scope. At a queried level, `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` is guarded only when every list operand references that level, so a fixed peer makes a mixed list unguarded; `NoGroupFilled` is unguarded whenever any operand references the level. Outside every captured prefix the leaf does not reference the level. The existing per-level `And`/`Or` rules then compose these leaf results.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`IterationNotValidVisitor.java`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/util/IterationNotValidVisitor.java) has distinct group-list branches: once the leaf references the queried level, `NoGroupFilled` rejects it, while `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` rejects when at least one operand does not reference that iteration. The existing checked star plan and rule loop establish that runtime reopens only the suffix below each complete captured outer environment. Current a12-dmkits group-star tests lock row counting and named-ancestry binding but do not state or separate this static mixed-operand admission rule.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Add the rule to the canonical §9 account and extend the existing group-star or ordinary-iteration law family with star-only positive admission, mixed fixed/star positive rejection, star-only negative rejection, an independent iterating `And` guard admitting the negative list, the same shape under `Or` remaining rejected, and a legal bound-prefix runtime control that binds by named ancestry and reports at the exact outer row. Reuse the existing condition, iteration, and group-star owners; add no visitor port, evaluator, topology, or harness.
- **Compatibility:** Treating every positive starred list as guarded admits a mixed list that can fire on an empty current row. Treating every starred list as negative rejects the legal all-iterating positive form. A tree-wide emptiness bit cannot express the `And`/`Or` asymmetry, and positional binding can select the wrong cross-subtree row even after static admission is correct.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical peer prose states the leaf rule; maintained kernel and JVM/Node cases separate all five admission shapes plus exact bound-prefix runtime selection; the existing owners remain singular; and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision and per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-26-02 — a nonrepeatable group below starred ancestors retains group-product semantics

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `45d24b73549124bf7da342a1ee19401408438fc6`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`02-logic-and-formal-errors.md` §A.4](../spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md#a4-fill-quantifiers-group-scopes-and-the-two-iteration-ranges) and [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §5](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#5-groupfilled-and-the-other-repetition-rules)
- **Delta:** A path such as `Sections*/Items*/Details`, where `Details` is nonrepeatable, uses the first-star topology only to select complete ancestor environments. In each selected environment the terminal contributes the existing admitted-content × error × relevance group product. An instantiated starred ancestor is not terminal content; an admitted scalar or instantiated repeatable descendant fills the terminal, malformed-only content is unavailable, and structural addressing failure stays outside semantic UNKNOWN. This is distinct from a terminal repeatable `G*`, whose instantiated terminal rows are structural filled contributions, and it does not add a numeric `NumberOfFilledGroups` form for a nonrepeatable terminal.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`KontextListenBedingungCreator`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/creation/creators/bedingung/KontextListenBedingungCreator.java) and [`ExpandService`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/util/ExpandService.java) retain the group terminal while expanding its starred ancestry; [`RuntimeController`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java), [`GroupFillCache`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/GroupFillCache.java), and [`ValidationCache`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/ValidationCache.java) keep admitted content, propagated group presence, and rejected values distinct. Reviewed a12-dmkits IF193 controls already establish the nonrepeatable group product and descendant-row content, but no maintained case at the basis revision composes that product with an ancestor-star path.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Reuse the existing group-list operand, first-star environment selection, and `GroupPresenceState`; do not add a terminal selector, row-count special case, second topology, or new harness. Add one focused authoring/runtime family for a nonrepeatable terminal below at least one starred ancestor that separates ancestor-only emptiness, admitted scalar content, instantiated repeatable-descendant content, malformed-only unavailability, and a positive witness beside an unavailable peer. Confirm JVM and Node plus both kernel strategies where the current maintained route permits. Canonical prose should distinguish this composition from terminal-repeatable structural row count.
- **Compatibility:** Counting every selected ancestor environment as filled over-reports empty nonrepeatable terminals. Treating the terminal as a fixed singleton loses row identity and can mix descendants from different ancestor environments. Collapsing malformed-only or addressing failure to empty makes `NoGroupFilled` fire incorrectly.
- **Acceptance:** The peer retains one topology and one group-product owner, canonical prose states the terminal split, maintained cases separate the five accounts above, neighboring terminal-repeatable counts remain unchanged, and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision plus per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-26-03 — same-target computations flatten into one first-selected table

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `1106e2b5`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`09-computations.md` §§2 and 5](../spec/09-computations.md#5-where-a-computation-runs--scope-and-the-parallel-join)
- **Delta:** Code generation distributes each computation's common precondition into its alternatives and concatenates all computations targeting one field, in supplied order, into one per-field first-match method. A selected alternative ends the instance scan unconditionally, including when its operation stores no value. A false guard can fall through across computation boundaries, but “first non-empty computation outcome wins” is false when an earlier selected operation produces no stored value.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`CalculationDependencies`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-calc/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/calc/internal/util/CalculationDependencies.java) preserves the per-target encounter-ordered information list, [`CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/codegen/internal/generator/backingbeans/calc/CodeGenCalculatedFieldBuilder.java) distributes common preconditions and concatenates every alternative, and [`CodeGenCalculationAlternative`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st) continues the target-instance loop after a selected operation, poison, or runtime failure. Existing a12-dmkits `MultiComputationFieldDiffTest` cases are Number-typed and make their no-value-first case unselected through a false guard, so none distinguishes this account from its current first-nonempty prose and interpreter mechanism.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Correct the canonical §11 account, IF87 finding, and computation engine to the single flattened first-selected mechanism. Add a String-target differential in which the first supplied computation selects an unguarded bare copy of an empty String operand and the second supplies a nonempty literal: the target must remain without a stored result because the first selection ended the scan. Retain the current false-guard fall-through case as the opposite control, and cover the split Groovy route if its maintained test surface can force the same selected-empty discriminator above the chunk threshold.
- **Compatibility:** Outcome-based first-nonempty dedup lets a later computation overwrite a selected-but-empty earlier result. Treating computation boundaries as unconditional stops instead breaks the already-observed false-guard fall-through. The corrected account keeps one selector and distinguishes selection from storage.
- **Acceptance:** Peer prose and interpreter use the flattened first-selected table; maintained JVM, Node, and both available kernel-strategy cases separate selected-empty stop from false-guard fall-through; the existing alternative selector remains the single semantic owner; and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision plus per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-26-04 — computed-target dependency poison erases producer cause

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `1106e2b5`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`09-computations.md` §3.2](../spec/09-computations.md#32-an-invalidity-clear-poisons)
- **Delta:** Once a computed target is invalid, later value and presence reads observe only invalid-target membership. The cascade carries no attempted payload, target-error subclass, originating formal cause, or message; the generated catch marks the dependent invalid and adds no cascade message. Producer details may remain in the producer's own result but cannot be recovered from the dependency read.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`CalculationCache`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/CalculationCache.java) stores invalid calculated identifiers in `invalidFields`; both `getWertXML` and `isEntityFilled` call `createCascadingError` from membership alone. [`CodeGenCalculationAlternative`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/calcDir/CodeGenCalculationAlternative.st) catches that exception, marks only the current target invalid, continues, and adds no calculation message in that branch. The existing peer `Poison`/overlay route appears cause-blind, but its canonical prose and maintained laws were not audited for an explicit no-provenance guarantee.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** State the cause-blind cascade rule in the canonical computation account and confirm that the existing `Poison`/overlay representation, JVM/Node interpreter laws, and kernel differentials do not preserve or reconstruct producer causes across a computed-target read. Add only the smallest representation-level or outcome-level lock needed to distinguish cause-blind poison from a provenance-carrying dependency; reuse the existing invalid-target and poison owners and add no message, trace, scheduler, or cause registry.
- **Compatibility:** Preserving a source formal cause or mapping a target rejection to a synthetic declared-constraint cause exposes information the kernel discarded and can make two otherwise identical cascades observably different to an analyzer. Collapsing poison to clean empty is also wrong: dependents must still abort when they actually read the invalid target.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical peer prose states cause erasure, the interpreter retains one cause-blind computed-target poison, focused laws or cases show that distinct invalid producer classes induce the same dependency observation while clean no-value remains distinct, and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision plus per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-26-05 — Number result change detection uses source V2 object identity

- **Status:** pending
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `0b4009ad251c3d63031d2efe804509f9f6c5ccf2`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`09-computations.md` §3.3](../spec/09-computations.md#33-what-compute-reports)
- **Delta:** Number change classification compares the original and computed V2 objects. Computed canonical Number output is a `BigDecimal`; an original `BigDecimal` is unchanged only when value and scale agree, while an original V2 `String` or negative-scale `BigDecimal` is always changed even when its plain text and numeric amount agree. Stored internal text and parsed numeric value cannot reconstruct this identity.
- **Basis:** Kernel [`DocumentComputationResultImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/a12internal/service/DocumentComputationResultImpl.java) converts computed internal text through `createValueV2` and compares it with the original document value through [`FieldInstanceImpl.fieldValuesEqual`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-a12internal/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/document/internal/service/impl/FieldInstanceImpl.java), which delegates ordinary values to `Objects.equals`. [`FormatDefinitionZahl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/formatdef/FormatDefinitionZahl.java), [`NumberUtil`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/NumberUtil.java), and [`ValidationData`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/internal/service/ValidationData.java) establish the source `String`/`BigDecimal` split and the lossy plain-text conversion of a typed decimal. The peer's `KernelComputeProjection` correctly keeps every Number VALUE for its raw String placement input, but its account says only that raw-placement profile and has no typed-decimal unchanged case.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Add the complete typed-source rule to the canonical computation account. Keep raw String-seeded Number placements always changed, and add a focused maintained result-projection seam or case for typed `BigDecimal` source values that separates equal coefficient-and-scale silence, `7` versus `7.00`, an equal-looking source String, and a negative-scale decimal with the same plain rendering. Reuse the existing computation-result projection; add no generic document or capture framework.
- **Compatibility:** Reconstructing from internal text incorrectly drops changes for source Strings and negative-scale decimals. Comparing parsed amount incorrectly drops `7` versus `7.00`; comparing text alone cannot recover either V2 object class or negative scale.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical peer prose states the V2 object-identity rule; maintained cases cover the four separators above through the narrowest existing result surface; raw-placement behavior remains unchanged; and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision plus per-surface disposition.

### SPEC-2026-07-25-01 — the `NotAll` fields-presence prepass is a size-selected route detail, not the observable contract

- **Status:** accepted — reviewed a12-dmkits revision `dd02b033`
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `7d17733a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`06-strings-and-enumerations.md` §B.3](../spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md)
- **Delta:** the clause stated unconditionally that `NotAll` "first checks whether the fields side has any present cell" and that "with none it does not fire without consuming the values side." That sequence holds on only one of two emission routes. The corrected clause states the values-then-fields form as the contract and demotes the prepass to an optimization detail that is observationally equivalent, with the route condition and the equivalence argument recorded. The companion Lean modelling note and checklist line are scoped to match.
- **Basis:** the codegen bean for field/value-list conditions selects its condition-name suffix by an optimization predicate, and the two suffixes reach different runtime methods. The ordinary method performs the prepass, then the values side, then a restarted fields scan. The optimized method has no prepass and a single fields scan, and its condition-parameter initialization template hoists the values side ahead of the whole condition, so the values side is always consumed. The predicate requires the fields side to have an operand with at least ten iterations while the values side has no filter, no iteration, and an operand list of at least twenty members.
- **Why the routes are nonetheless equivalent:** the values-side membership test counts only cells marked as given, so with no present fields cell the un-prepassed route reports the same collapsed non-firing result as the prepass would. The prepass is a short-circuit, not a semantic gate. This is why the correction narrows the *claim* rather than reporting a behavioral divergence.
- **Please also note, as coverage information rather than a request:** the case in your `ValueListQuantifierOrderDiffTest` that reads as confirming this prepass asserts non-firing for both the prepass scenario and its present-fields control, comparing fired-code sets. Both accounts predict both outcomes, so that case locks tri-engine agreement but cannot separate prepass from no-prepass. The same file is admirably explicit about a *different* blindness — its note that the kernel merges false and unknown in the validation result, making that ordering interpreter-tier only — and the disposition of [`SPEC-2026-07-23-16`](#spec-2026-07-23-16--value-list-quantifiers-preserve-operator-specific-encounter-order) carries that qualification but not this one. No change is requested to the test; we would simply not want either project to cite it as separating this distinction.
- **Also worth knowing:** no fixture in either corpus reaches either optimization threshold, so the optimized route is unexercised on both sides. We are not requesting a probe, because we could not construct a separating input pair. One residue we did not chase, recorded in case you can settle it cheaply by reading: the two routes source their numeric-kind decision differently, one probing the entity list at runtime and one receiving a codegen constant, which a mixed-kind value list could separate if authoring admits one. The entity-list checker appears to enforce kind agreement, but we did not verify how completely.
- **Acceptance:** reconcile the narrowed claim into your value-list semantic prose so neither project restates the prepass as unconditional; adjust the citation scope of the affected test if you agree it does not separate this distinction; and return the exact reviewed revision plus a per-surface disposition.
- **Disposition (reviewed a12-dmkits `dd02b033`): accepted on every surface, with the residue settled and one local error corrected.** The canonical peer clause now states the route-independent contract with the prepass demoted to a parenthetical and an explicit prohibition on restating it as the contract; a new findings supplement carries the route predicate and equivalence argument; the affected test's header records that it must not be cited as separating the distinction, with no logic changed; and `IG87` records the optimized route as unexercised on both sides with a re-open trigger.
- **The residue this entry handed over is closed, and the correction runs against this entry's own text.** This entry described the two routes as "one probing the entity list at runtime and one receiving a codegen constant." That is wrong: **both are runtime probes, of different operand lists** — the ordinary route probes the first *fields* operand, while the optimized route's initialization template emits a hoisted probe of the first *values* operand. The separator is unauthorable rather than merely unexercised: the entity-list checker takes its expected base type from the first fields operand and matches the values side against it, on top of checking base-type agreement within the fields side, so one base type spans both lists. Base-type agreement is strictly finer than the number-versus-not question the probe asks, so the two probes cannot disagree. This closes the last route-divergence risk for the family and means one clause remains correct — no route split is needed in `spec/` or in Lean.
- **Returned with the acceptance, as coverage information rather than a request:** the correction is safe as a statement about the engine and unsafe as implementation guidance, and this repository made exactly that mistake before catching it. Its own modelling note went on to advise consuming the values side unconditionally; that would be wrong for both projects, because both classify the values side eagerly. In this repository's `evalOrdered` and in your `notAllRes`, the presence guard is reached before values-side poison is computed, so removing it returns UNKNOWN where the contract requires non-firing for a fields side with no present cell. The kernel's un-prepassed route is safe only because it applies poison at fire time. Consequence worth checking on your side: your narrowed clause is correct, but three comments in `ValueListQuantEval` still describe the prepass in route terms ("values NEVER consumed", "never touches values when its presence prepass decided"), so a future reader reconciling the comments to the new clause could remove a load-bearing guard. Local fix recorded as [`LF72`](LEAN-FINDINGS.md#lf72--demoting-a-route-detail-does-not-make-it-removable-an-eager-decomposition-can-make-the-guard-carry-the-contract); no change is requested and no spec clause moved.

### SPEC-2026-07-25-02 — the below-half separator is real, but it is a parse-time mechanism rather than a `Math.round` one

- **Status:** accepted — reviewed a12-dmkits revision `97e35531`; the correction landed and returned three corrections of its own
- **Kind:** semantic correction
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `10451733`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1; the disputed half is host-JDK-dependent rather than kernel-version-dependent
- **Canonical clause:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §1](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#1-when-a-rule-iterates-and-where-its-error-lands)
- **Delta:** the clause said only that "a decimal immediately below one half can round onto one half", without naming which rounding does it, positioned directly after `Math.round`. That wording invited the wrong reading and is now split into the two distinct mechanisms, with the historical `floor(x + 0.5)` behaviour named and explicitly excluded.
- **Your correction is adopted in part, and the other part would delete a real separator.** Adopted: `Math.round` does not lift a double strictly below one half on any supported host, so nothing should reproduce the pre-Java-7 quirk; and its half-up-toward-positive-infinity direction gives the asymmetry you measured, which is now stated in the clause. Not adopted: dropping the separator, because a decimal strictly below one half genuinely can convert to a nonzero integer — by **binary64 tie-to-even at parse time**, before `Math.round` is reached.
- **The separating witness, evaluated through this repository's conversion owner:** `1/2 − 2⁻⁵⁵` is exactly halfway between the two neighbouring doubles, ties to `0.5`, and converts to **1**; the decimal one ulp below it converts to **0**; and your `0.49999999999999994` also converts to **0**, because it parses to the double strictly below one half. So the two accounts agree on your literal and disagree only about whether any below-half decimal can convert nonzero. One can — via a different mechanism than the one your check tested.
- **Why your fixtures cannot see it, which is why this is a correction rather than a probe request:** the witness needs an authored scale of about 55 digits, which the authoring scale gate rejects before the admission visitor runs — the same boundary your handback already documented for long saturation and the `2^32` wrap. This is therefore a conversion-level fact on both sides, and no probe is requested.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** narrow the finding so it states that `Math.round` does not lift a below-half double *and* that parse-time tie-to-even can still move a below-half decimal onto one half. As committed, "the below-half separator does not exist on JDK 7+" is true of the rounding step but reads as a claim about the conversion as a whole.
- **Compatibility:** an implementation that rounds the *decimal* rather than its binary64 image will classify the tie witness as zero and diverge on static admission, while an implementation that reproduces the pre-Java-7 lift will diverge on yours. Both mechanisms must be present and distinct. Because the excluded behaviour is a platform property, a compatibility claim should name the host JDK generation rather than only the kernel version.
- **Acceptance:** the peer finding distinguishes the two mechanisms; neither project's prose implies that every below-half decimal converts to zero; and the handback supplies the exact reviewed revision plus its disposition.
- **Disposition (reviewed a12-dmkits `97e35531`): accepted, generalized, and corrected in three places — two of them mine.** They reproduced the witness through the kernel's own conversion and generalized the mechanism correctly: binary64 round-to-nearest-even lifts **every** decimal in `[½ − 2⁻⁵⁵, ½)` onto one half, because that interval is ties between `0.5` (even significand) and `0.5 − 2⁻⁵⁴` (odd). Their canonical finding now separates the parse step from the rounding step, and they withdrew "drop the separator".
- **Their refinement, adopted:** the lift is **positive-side only**. A lifted `−0.5` still converts to zero, so the ±0.5 asymmetry survives the parse step rather than being softened by it.
- **Correction to this entry (1): the witness needs 17 fractional digits, not ~55.** This entry cited `1/2 − 2⁻⁵⁵`, whose *exact* decimal is 55 digits, and mistook that for the authoring requirement. Any decimal in the interval lifts, and the shortest is 17 digits; at sixteen or fewer the interval holds none strictly below one half. That collapses the unreachability margin from about forty decimal places to **three**, which is a materially different claim.
- **Correction to this entry (2), since RETRACTED upstream — recorded in place rather than deleted.** As written it said: the wall is the field scale cap, not the literal gate; a Number field's maximum fractional digits caps at 14 and a comparison literal may carry at most the field's scale, so `14 < 17` makes the witness unauthorable on **every kernel-legal model**. The cap and the literal rule are both real and both retained. **The conclusion is not.** The peer withdrew it at `724926a5`: the literal gate is `MVK_INVALID_COMPARE_DEC_PLACES`, which is the kernel's **one suppressible code**, so the third link is defeated by a first-class authored directive. Against a scale-14 field with the gate suppressed, `== 0.49999999999999998` is admitted and `== 0.49999999999999990` is rejected — equal-length literals differing in the final digit, straddling the whole partition. `spec/07` is restored to an unqualified clause and a conformance case now carries that authorable pair in place of the unauthorable 55/56-digit fixture. **The aggravating detail:** the defeater was already canonical in this repository's own [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md`](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md), which states the suppression "allows an `==`/`!=` across differing scales" and is the only suppressible code, with its grammar in [`12-concrete-syntax.md`](../spec/12-concrete-syntax.md). This was not missing evidence; it was unread evidence, in the canonical prose this project owns. See [`LF75`](LEAN-FINDINGS.md).
- **Correction inbound to this repository (3), and the more consequential one: `2^32` is authorable after all.** Their earlier handback said long saturation and the signed-32-bit wrap need a literal the decimal-places gate rejects first. That was **inference**, now withdrawn and measured: the gate compares *fractional* digits, so a scale-0 field admits a bare integer literal and it reaches the classifier on its narrowed value — `== 4294967296` rejected, `== 4294967297` admitted, `2^63` admitted through long saturation. This repository had adopted the inference into [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md`](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md) as a conversion-level fact; the clause now states the ±1 pair straddling `2^32` as the sharpest reachable separator. The local Lean was already correct and its conformance pair already covered it. **Process note: this is the second time in one session that a peer *inference* was adopted here as measured fact.** The hypothesis rule already covers it — reachability is exactly the claim class it names — so the failure was not asking which layer established it.
- **Also inbound:** the decimal-places gate is **at most**, not equality; a shorter literal is admitted and reaches the partition normally. Their diagnostic text says the digit counts "are different", which invites the equality reading. Recorded in the same clause.
- **Closed at reviewed a12-dmkits `724926a5`; nothing outbound.** That revision retracts correction (2) above, resolves the `getZahlKonstanteWert` `-1` sentinel hypothesis this exchange left open, and adds two facts adopted directly: the declared fractional interval `0 ≤ min ≤ max ≤ 14` (cap MVK-less, ordering `MVK_FRACT_DIGITS_INVALID`) now in [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md`](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md), and a second independent death for the table-enum region — the sole implementor's empty `getDependentColumns()` — now the tighter citation in [`06-strings-and-enumerations.md`](../spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md) and [`SG8`](SEMANTICS-GAPS.md#sg8--enumeration-and-value-list-completion). Both revisions are committed and reviewed upstream, so all of it lands as provenance in [`SOURCES.md`](SOURCES.md) and creates **no** outbound entry. Of the corrections exchanged under this entry, two were outbound and three inbound, and one of the inbound ones corrected an earlier inbound one — which is the argument for recording superseded reasoning in place rather than editing it away.

### SPEC-2026-07-25-03 — the long boundary classifies `+(2^63 - 1)` and `-(2^63 - 1)` oppositely, and only the negative side separates exact-integer arithmetic

- **Status:** accepted and closed — reviewed a12-dmkits `ee2f5d84`; all three predicted rows measured through the real kernel and confirmed
- **Kind:** semantic correction (addition), locally originated; **kernel-measured** as of `ee2f5d84`
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `724926a5`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1; the mechanism is binary64 representability, so it is host-JDK-independent
- **Canonical clause:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §1](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#1-when-a-rule-iterates-and-where-its-error-lands)
- **Delta:** your `724926a5` §4 locked `longSaturationNarrowsToMinusOneAndIsAdmitted` on the positive side only. The negative end classifies oppositely, and the cause is the long range's own asymmetry rather than the parse step. **Neither** `+(2^63 - 1)` nor `-(2^63 - 1)` is representable in binary64 — doubles just below `2^63` are spaced 1024 apart — so both parse onto `±2^63` and the parse step decides nothing here. The saturation does: `+2^63` exceeds `Long.MAX_VALUE` and saturates down to `2^63 - 1`, whose low 32 bits are all set; `-2^63` **is** `Long.MIN_VALUE` exactly, passes through unsaturated, and has them clear. Predicted classification on a scale-0 field in a repeatable group:

| condition | predicted | chain |
|---|---|---|
| `[Count] == 9223372036854775807` | **admitted** | → `+2^63` → saturates to `Long.MAX` → `-1` |
| `[Count] == -9223372036854775807` | **REJECTED** | → `-2^63` = `Long.MIN`, unsaturated → `0` |
| `[Count] == -9223372036854773760` | **admitted** | representable exactly; residue `2048` |

- **Why this pair is worth locking even though you already lock the positive one.** It is the sharpest available separator against **exact 64-bit integer arithmetic**, which is what a reimplementation in Rust, Python, or Kotlin `BigInteger` writes by default: computing `-(2^63 - 1) mod 2^32` exactly gives `1`, so that implementation **admits** a condition the kernel rejects. Your positive row cannot catch this, because exact arithmetic and the binary64 route agree at `+(2^63 - 1)`. The third row is a required control — without it the pair reads as "the negative boundary always rejects", which is false.
- **A second, weaker account it also separates:** clamping to the signed-32-bit range instead of saturating the long first. That agrees with the correct account at `2^63` and contradicts it at the negative boundary.
- **Basis and confidence.** The three rows are computed by this repository's conversion owner and independently reproduced against a separate IEEE-754 implementation, and they agree with every measurement you have supplied in this family. They remain a *derivation*, not an observation, which is why the entry is pending.
- **How this was found, which is the reason it is marked pending rather than asserted.** This repository predicted a `±1` pair at the negative boundary by reasoning on the exact decimal, wrote it as a conformance case, and the case **failed** — because the prediction skipped the parse step, which is the same mechanism this whole exchange has been about. The corrected rows above are what our conversion owner actually computes. They are therefore a model prediction that reproduces every measurement you have supplied so far, not an independent observation.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** measure the three conditions above through the real kernel. If they hold, the clause is confirmed and you may want the `-(2^63 - 1)` row beside your existing saturation case, since it is the one that catches exact-integer reimplementations. If any differs, this repository's conversion owner is wrong at the negative boundary and the clause plus its conformance rows are corrected here.
- **Riding along, so the family closes in one run rather than another round trip:** your `724926a5` measurement shows a 19-digit comparison literal is admitted at model check, so the 15-digit input bound does not gate authored literals in that position. What that bound *does* constrain is unestablished on both sides; [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md`](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md) now says only what was measured and explicitly declines to state its scope. If it is cheap to determine while measuring the rows above, it closes the last open question in this family.
- **Compatibility:** an implementation reproducing this needs the parse step at *both* ends of the long range, not only near one half. Rounding the decimal, or doing exact integer arithmetic, diverges on static admission for a literal a model may legally author.
- **Acceptance:** the three conditions are measured on a scale-0 field in a repeatable group, and the outcome is recorded with its revision — including a refutation, which corrects this repository rather than the peer.
