- **Disposition:** accepted with an interpreter correction — commit `affde9e4`, strengthened by `e3db6c30`, carries complete starred extent through the existing `starRelevant` seam. Incomplete extent suppresses `No` on either side and values-side `NotAll`; `AtLeastOne` and fields-side `NotAll` retain their established behavior. Full validation is unchanged, and wildcard, concrete-row, relevant-ancestor, side, strategy, JVM, and Node controls are locked.

### SPEC-2026-07-22-10 — `RepetitionNotUnique` compares every non-Number key by stored text

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `83dd514f9283b9f62dbe6ee6f238e5c67a00e9c6`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §6](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#6-repetitionnotunique-precisely-)
- **Delta:** Complete RNU key admission and equality. The nonempty key list accepts direct plain value-validating fields without a Number/String-only kind restriction; it rejects groups, categories, wildcards, semantic indices, and String fields configured without value validation. Number keys use scale-19 numeric equality, while every non-Number key uses exact internally stored text. Direct Enumeration therefore compares stored tokens rather than display/category projections, and temporal RNU does not inherit instant equality.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckWiederholungBedingungImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/bedingung/CheckWiederholungBedingungImpl.java) applies field/category/wildcard/semantic-index/value-validation gates but no field-kind gate; [`CheckEntityUtils.checkIsValueValidation`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/CheckEntityUtils.java) rejects only the no-value-validation profile at that step. [`RuntimeController.getWerteOrNullIfEinFeldNichtPruefungsRelevant`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) special-cases Number as `VkBigDecimal` and passes every other kind through exact `feldWertIntern` String equality; its nearby Number/String-only Javadoc is stale. At the a12-dmkits basis revision, [`RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/RepetitionNotUniqueEvaluator.kt) already appears mechanism-aligned—Number gets `rnuNumericKey`, every other declared kind gets `KeyAtom.Exact`—but maintained RNU differentials do not lock the wider admission or Enumeration/temporal stored-identity separators.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Review the existing typed RNU authoring and evaluator against the complete direct-field admission contract, correct only any actual admission gap, and update the canonical §9 owner. Reuse the existing `RepetitionNotUniqueCondition`, field references, formal-cell gate, and two-branch `KeyAtom` mechanism. Add focused authoring and maintained dual-kernel-route cases for a direct Enumeration key, a category-collision control proving stored-token identity, and a temporal pair that separates equal parsed/instant meaning from different stored text where the model surface permits it; retain Number normalization, String exactness, Boolean/Confirm admission, formal-invalid exclusion, optional-empty polarity, and category/wildcard/semantic-index/no-value-validation rejections. Do not add a second evaluator, generic raw-value framework, protocol, or harness.
- **Compatibility:** Restricting RNU to Number/String rejects legal models. Reusing Enumeration category projection, display equality, or ordinary temporal instant equality merges rows the kernel keeps distinct; conversely, parsing every non-Number to a semantic value can erase a stored-spelling distinction. The correction must not weaken formal key validation or change Number normalization.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node interpreter admit the verified direct non-Number kinds, compare Enumeration and temporal witnesses by exact stored text, preserve Number scale-19 equality and existing empty/invalid/polarity/cluster behavior, reject the forbidden reference shapes at the existing static boundary, update canonical documentation, and return the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Disposition:** accepted; implementation already satisfied — commit `92e1c712`, audited by `e3db6c30`, documents and locks direct value-validating admission for every supported kind, scale-19 Number equality, and exact stored-text equality for every non-Number kind. Enumeration token identity is represented directly. Fixed-width temporal formats provide no authorable same-instant/different-spelling witness, so temporal stored-text identity is documented structurally instead of with a fabricated firing case.

- **Status:** accepted

- **Delta:** A pure validation/computation call is parameterized by the complete admitted processing context, not only a clock and locale. The context retains the effective error-message locale, the resolved current instant/date including the configured test override or one host sample, custom-condition semantics, custom-field-type semantics, and deprecated additional information while compatibility with that 30.8.1 surface is claimed. Model-owned time-zone rules remain another explicit pure input. A narrower product may reject an unsupported capability before evaluation but must not silently omit it from a broader compatibility claim.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`DocumentProcessingConfig`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/api/DocumentProcessingConfig.java) exposes `languageOfErrorMessages`, `customConditionFactory`, `customFieldTypeFactory`, deprecated `additionalInformation`, and `currentDateForTest`; the last selects the date used by `Today` instead of the actual current date. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, the interpreter exposes `EvalClock`, custom conditions, and custom field types through separate capabilities, while its permanent guidance lists the three active factory/locale members and notes that `additionalInformation` is deprecated without stating whether the legacy semantic input is represented or excluded from its compatibility claim.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Reconcile the canonical processing-context description and compatibility claims with the complete 30.8.1 input set. Existing separate `EvalClock`, custom-condition, custom-field-type, locale, and model-zone carriers may remain separate when their semantics are coherent; do not add a generic context framework solely to mirror the Java object. State any intentionally unsupported legacy additional-information behavior as a compatibility exclusion rather than silently dropping it. Add or change implementation/tests only if the review finds an actual observable gap.
- **Compatibility:** This primarily completes the semantic compatibility envelope. It does not require Java factory objects in a clean-room interpreter, but a product that omits a supported configuration input cannot claim the corresponding complete V2 behavior. Deprecated V1 or legacy-host compatibility remains a separate claim.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical a12-dmkits documentation names every observable processing input or explicitly excludes it from the claimed product fragment, preserves deterministic injected clock and custom-semantic behavior, introduces no duplicate runtime context or ambient host lookup, and returns the exact reviewed revision plus per-surface disposition.

Deprecated `additionalInformation` is explicitly excluded from the claimed clean-room compatibility fragment; no generic context framework was introduced.

### SPEC-2026-07-22-12 — computation eagerly validates formal operands without globally aborting

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `83dd514f9283b9f62dbe6ee6f238e5c67a00e9c6`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`01-data-model.md` §4.3](../spec/01-data-model.md#43-the-compute--apply--validate-flow), [`09-computations.md` §3.3](../spec/09-computations.md#33-what-compute-reports), and the [`SEMANTICS-MAP.md` §11 summary](../spec/SEMANTICS-MAP.md#3-the-taxonomy)
- **Delta:** Before ordinary computation processing, V2 computation formally validates every non-computed field in the source-defined computation-operand set. Invalid filled values are reported; ordinary required-empty omission errors are not reported in this prepass; empty or non-unique index fields are reported. Processing continues after collection, and the eager public inventory remains separate from dependency-sensitive skipping or reached-read poison. A represented erroneous computed instance is removed from the source-filled residual and reports through the computed-instance-error channel instead of also appearing as CLEARED; only remaining source-filled instances become cleared.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`IDocumentRtService.compute`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/api/IDocumentRtService.java) documents the prepass cases, continued processing, and separate result channel. [`IDocumentComputationResult`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/api/IDocumentComputationResult.java) distinguishes erroneous, cleared, and formal-operand channels. [`DocumentComputationResultImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/a12internal/service/DocumentComputationResultImpl.java) removes a represented computed pointer from the originally filled map before branching on its error, then projects residual pointers to cleared and unmatched messages to formal operand errors. The exact operand extraction boundary and the reachability/classification of an error paired with an empty computed payload remain SG4 audit questions rather than facts inferred from this construction.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Reconcile this follow-up after [`SPEC-2026-07-22-08`](#spec-2026-07-22-08--computation-results-retain-rich-v2-projections-beyond-the-change-delta) so the eager-prepass account reuses the settled result-channel terminology. Document its invalid/required-empty/index cases in the canonical computation owner, preserving its separation from runtime reachability and the report-all interpreter contract. Review the existing compute preparation and result projection for an actual behavior gap; reuse those owners and existing differential routes if a correction is needed. Do not introduce a scheduler, second validation pass, result framework, protocol, or new harness.
- **Compatibility:** Treating every required empty as a formal operand error, globally aborting on any collected error, folding operand errors into computed-target errors, or reporting one represented erroneous target as both ERRORED and CLEARED changes observable V2 behavior. The exact dependency response remains a separate scheduling claim and is not settled by the service Javadoc's broad skip wording.
- **Acceptance:** a12-dmkits canonical semantics states the complete eager-prepass contract and the three distinct error/cleared channels, its current implementation boundary is honest, focused maintained controls distinguish required-empty silence, invalid-value reporting, index errors, continued unrelated computation, and ERRORED-versus-CLEARED ownership where supported, no duplicate mechanism is added, and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Disposition:** accepted with wording correction; implementation was already folded in — commit `f765936e`, corrected by `e3db6c30`, locks invalid filled operands, silent ordinary required-empty operands, empty/non-unique index errors, continued computation processing, and ERRORED-versus-CLEARED ownership. The source proves an eager source-defined operand inventory before ordinary computation processing, not a separately observable dependency-scheduling contract.

### SPEC-2026-07-22-13 — `FieldValueAsNumber` checks the complete selected source domain

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `affde9e49b17e1d82812e106676613fad4b94ff1`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §4](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#4-other-numeric-constraints)
- **Delta:** Complete the existing `FieldValueAsNumber` account. A String source requires value validation, exact pattern `[0-9]+`, and finite maximum length at most 15, giving scale 0. An ordinary Enumeration may select stored or named-category tokens; every token in that selected domain must satisfy the decimal grammar and 15-digit budget, and the result scale is the domain's maximum fractional length. The decimal grammar admits optional leading minus, at most one point, a leading point, and host decimal digits, but no trailing point, plus, exponent, whitespace, or comma. Runtime projects the exact stored/category token before conversion. Missing is numeric zero with both validation movement directions; present is fixed; formal unavailability remains unavailable/poison.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckFeldOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckFeldOpImpl.java) and [`CheckEntityUtils`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/CheckEntityUtils.java) own source-kind, pattern, maximum-length, complete selected-domain, digit-budget, and derived-scale admission. [`RuntimeController`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java), [`ValidationCache`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/ValidationCache.java), and [`VkBigDecimal`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/VkBigDecimal.java) establish selected-token lookup, exact decimal conversion, empty zero, and formal propagation. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, `KERNEL-FINDINGS.md` family 4, `MVK-LEDGER.md`, `FieldValueCoercionDiffTest`, and the interpreter already own the basic numeric-Enumeration and empty-zero behavior, but do not state or separate the complete String/category lexical, scale, and non-ASCII digit profile.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Preserve the existing AST, evaluator, `MVK_NO_CONVERT_IN_NUMBER` delegation, and maintained coercion differential. Extend the canonical §5 account with the complete admission/result-scale contract and audit the typed authoring plus JVM/Node decimal parser against stored and category domains. Add focused controls for a legal category projection, leading point/minus, 15-versus-16 digits, rejected trailing point/plus/exponent/whitespace/comma, the exact String pattern/maximum-length gate, derived fractional scale, symmetric missing polarity, and one non-ASCII decimal-digit witness. Change implementation only for an observed peer divergence; do not add another numeric AST, parser framework, or harness.
- **Compatibility:** An ASCII-only parser, stored-domain check applied before category selection, permissive free-text String admission, wrong result scale, or one-directional empty fillability can change authoring, exact-scale comparison, firing polarity, generated validation, and computation results. The current Lean capsule deliberately implements only the ordinary closed Enumeration/category ASCII subset and leaves String plus non-ASCII digits open pending their checked representation and focused evidence.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer agree on the lexical, domain-selection, digit-budget, result-scale, empty-polarity, and formal-propagation separators; canonical prose records the full contract; existing basic coercion controls remain green; no duplicate conversion mechanism is added; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Disposition:** accepted with authoring and runtime fixes — commit `e3db6c30` validates the complete selected stored/category domain, enforces the String pattern and finite-length contract, derives scale from that domain, accepts the kernel's host decimal-digit grammar including non-ASCII decimal digits, retains the 15-digit budget, and preserves missing-value and formal-poison behavior. The existing AST and evaluator remain the owners; previously rejected kernel-legal inputs now succeed.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-01 — direct numeric wrappers admit `FieldValueAsNumber` sources

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `affde9e49b17e1d82812e106676613fad4b94ff1`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §2 and §4](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#2-rounding) and [`09-computations.md` §1](../spec/09-computations.md#1-the-ground-rules)
- **Delta:** The operation-form `RoundDown`, `RoundUp`, `RoundAccounting`, and `Abs` wrappers admit a direct `FieldValueAsNumber(...)` child in validation and computation. Their shared static check rejects only an immediate literal or bracketed-literal child and requires an already-derived number-like result; it does not require the whole child subtree to be constant-free. Rounding replaces result scale with the authored places, defaults omission to 0, and preserves directional fillability. `Abs` retains the conversion's derived scale and transforms fillability by the current sign, making symmetric missing zero grow-only in magnitude. Computation maps clean missing through either wrapper to numeric zero and preserves exact formal unavailability as poison.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckRundenOperOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckRundenOperOpImpl.java) and [`CheckFeldOperOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckFeldOperOpImpl.java) apply the same immediate-child nonconstant and number-like checks to operation-form rounding and `Abs`; [`CheckOpUtils`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/CheckOpUtils.java) shows that `isConstant` recognizes only a direct or bracketed literal; the result-type writes assign authored rounding scale or preserve the child type respectively. The runtime numeric helpers apply the existing wrapper transforms after `FieldValueAsNumber` conversion. This follow-up was discovered after [`SPEC-2026-07-22-13`](#spec-2026-07-22-13--fieldvalueasnumber-checks-the-complete-selected-source-domain) entered upstream work, so that immutable in-flight entry remains unchanged.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Reuse the existing numeric AST, wrapper evaluator, authoring checker, and `FieldValueAsNumber` implementation. Confirm that both wrapper forms accept the conversion directly in validation and computation, retain the stated scale/fillability/poison projections, and add the smallest maintained cross-product controls if the combination is not already locked. Correct documentation or implementation only for an observed divergence. Do not introduce a wrapper-specific conversion node, second numeric dispatch, new harness, or recursively stronger constant prohibition.
- **Compatibility:** Rejecting the direct combination, copying symmetric missing fillability through `Abs`, retaining the source scale after rounding, recursively rejecting a legal child merely because its wider subtree contains a constant, or erasing formal poison changes authoring, exact-scale gates, validation polarity, and computed results.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer agree on direct rounding/`Abs` admission over stored and category `FieldValueAsNumber`, authored versus omitted rounding scale, missing-source polarity for both wrappers, exact present values, and formal poison; existing standalone conversion and wrapper controls remain green; no duplicate semantic mechanism is added; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Disposition:** accepted; the shared conversion fixes were sufficient — commit `e3db6c30` adds cross-product tests and documentation for direct rounding and `Abs` over stored/category `FieldValueAsNumber`, including authored/default scale, missing polarity, present values, poison, both kernel strategies, and JVM/Node execution. No wrapper-specific runtime change was needed.

- **Disposition:** accepted with correction — `TemporalNumericWrapperDiffTest` and matching interpreter controls lock stored components, Base-Year-derived components, signed Date differences, generated validation, computation, empty polarity, and poison across both kernel strategies plus JVM/Node. The requested direct-`BaseYear` rejection premise was false: both kernel strategies admit `RoundDown(BaseYear)` and `Abs(BaseYear)`. Commit `27afb555` fixes the peer typed `Abs` gate while reusing its existing numeric Base-Year and wrapper mechanisms; the local canonical clauses and Lean source admission now reject literal syntax rather than semantically fixed numeric sources.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-05 — numeric operation wrappers compose over admitted value functions

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §4](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#4-other-numeric-constraints), [`09-computations.md` §1](../spec/09-computations.md#1-the-ground-rules), and the [`SEMANTICS-MAP.md` §5 summary](../spec/SEMANTICS-MAP.md#3-the-taxonomy)
- **Delta:** Operation-form `RoundDown`, `RoundUp`, `RoundAccounting`, and `Abs` accept another admitted numeric wrapper or operand-list `Min`/`Max` as their child. Each wrapper independently rejects only an immediate or bracketed numeric literal after requiring a number-like child result. Nested wrappers evaluate from the innermost outward in authored order; an outer rounding operation assigns its authored scale, while outer `Abs` preserves the completed child scale and applies its sign-sensitive directional transform. Arithmetic domain failure and exact formal unavailability survive every stage. The enclosing legacy authoring walk descends through each reached value-function subtree for division-region checks, while every function node separates an outer direct-left-power relation.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckRundenOperOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckRundenOperOpImpl.java) and [`CheckFeldOperOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckFeldOperOpImpl.java) reject only `CheckOpUtils.isConstant` after the child has acquired a number-like type; the operation grammar accepts nested operations. [`CheckOperationImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckOperationImpl.java) walks reached function subtrees for the enclosing division restriction but recognizes a forbidden outer nested power only when the direct left child is itself a power. The established runtime value functions apply their transformations to the completed child result and preserve invalidity. At the reviewed a12-dmkits revision, `Rounding` and `OpListMath` already retain arbitrary `Calculation` children, the evaluator composes them recursively, the catalog describes arbitrary-expression admission, and `DivisionDomainFailureComputationTest` locks nested `RoundAccounting(Abs(...))` plus rounding over `Min`/`Max` domain failure.
- **a12-dmkits reconciliation:** none requested. The peer already represents the corrected canonical contract through its existing AST, evaluator, public DSL, documentation, and focused computation controls. This accepted receipt records a source-audited Lean/spec expansion without sending already-satisfied work back upstream.
- **Compatibility:** Rejecting nested wrappers or an admitted extremum loses legal expressions. Flattening nested stages changes results such as `RoundDown(Abs(-1.4)) = 1` versus `Abs(RoundDown(-1.4)) = 2`. Stopping division traversal at a wrapper, extending an outer direct-left-power rejection through it, or converting domain failure/formal poison to clean zero changes authoring and computation behavior.
- **Acceptance:** The Lean checked source, validation, computation, and generated-validation routes reuse one recursive unary evaluator and the existing direct-extremum fold; focused cases separate wrapper order, immediate-literal rejection, extremum consumption, enclosing division traversal, domain failure, target references, and generated validation; existing generic delegation/totality laws remain trusted; a12-dmkits requires no duplicate change; and project-local retained kernel correspondence remains pending under SG12.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Disposition:** accepted as already satisfied upstream — no a12-dmkits implementation or documentation request is open for this entry.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-06 — numeric operand-list extrema preserve per-call boundaries

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §4](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#4-other-numeric-constraints) and the [`SEMANTICS-MAP.md` §5 summary](../spec/SEMANTICS-MAP.md#3-the-taxonomy)
- **Delta:** Numeric operand-list `Min`/`Max` accepts a nonempty ordered list of complete number-like operations and composes inside surrounding numeric arithmetic. Each call independently permits at most one immediate or bracketed numeric literal. A literal nested inside arithmetic or another value-function call does not consume the outer call's budget, but its own enclosing call still checks it. Consequently `Min(Min([A], 1), 2)` is admitted while `Min([A], 1, 2)` is rejected; nested same-operator calls must not be flattened. Static scale is the union/maximum across the complete immediate operands and multiplicative-constant capability survives only when every operand supplies it. Runtime evaluates and folds operands from left to right in authored order.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: the grammar's `feldListenOperOp` consumes `operationListe`, so every list member is a complete operation and each nested call remains a distinct parse-tree node. [`CheckFeldListenOperOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckFeldListenOperOpImpl.java) applies its constant count, number-like check, scale union, and all-operands constant capability to one call's immediate operation list. [`CheckOpUtils.isConstant`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/CheckOpUtils.java) recognizes only an immediate literal or recursively bracketed literal. [`OpListOperationImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/impl/operation/OpListOperationImpl.java), code-generation `OpListOperation`, and `RuntimeController.combineOperatorListe` preserve list/call boundaries and authored order through the runtime fold. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, `OpListMath` already retains arbitrary `Calculation` operands, `Dsl.min`/`max` preserve each nested list node, the renderer recursively emits them, and the interpreter evaluates the list in order. `MVK-LEDGER.md` states only the weaker one-constant rule and no focused maintained law separates grouped, arithmetic-nested, nested-call, and flattened constants.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Preserve the existing `OpListMath`, renderer, parser/read route, evaluator, and kernel-delegated admission strategy. Extend the canonical numeric authoring account and the smallest existing admission-law/differential family to lock one direct literal, one bracketed literal, two direct literals rejected, a literal inside arithmetic plus one direct literal admitted, nested same- and different-selector calls with one literal per call admitted, and a completed call inside surrounding arithmetic. Audit static scale/capability wording and existing runtime tests, changing implementation only for an observed divergence. Do not add another AST, list evaluator, constant scanner, pre-parser, harness, or normalization pass.
- **Compatibility:** Flattening nested calls, recursively counting every descendant literal against the outer budget, treating braces as hiding a literal, restricting operands to direct fields, or rejecting surrounding arithmetic changes legal-model admission. Reordering operands or rebuilding scale/capability from a flattened tree can additionally change staged execution, authoring diagnostics, exact-scale gates, and computation results.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer agree on the direct/grouped/arithmetic-nested/nested-call/flattened separating matrix; canonical prose records the per-call rule; the existing list-preserving typed AST, renderer, and evaluator remain the sole owners; no duplicate admission or runtime mechanism is added; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `434f4ec8585797677d032639c50b491172bbbf54` (primary admission locks at `8a9b9903`; catalog strengthening at `193e279a`)
- **Disposition:** accepted — the peer implementation already preserved independent `OpListMath` call nodes and evaluation order. Canonical prose now states the per-call literal budget, and the maintained admission matrix plus kernel-executed catalog invalid examples separate direct, grouped, arithmetic-nested, nested-call, and flattened forms without adding another scanner or evaluator.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-07 — numeric Enumeration conversion uses the host UTF-16 digit class

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §4](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#4-other-numeric-constraints)
- **Delta:** Clarify the accepted `FieldValueAsNumber` decimal grammar from SPEC-2026-07-22-13. The kernel tests each UTF-16 `char` with Java `Character.isDigit(char)`: non-ASCII BMP decimal digits such as Arabic-Indic and fullwidth digits are admitted and converted by their decimal value, but a supplementary-plane decimal code point is rejected because Commons Lang iterates its two surrogate code units separately. This is narrower than applying `Character.isDigit(int)` or a Unicode-scalar decimal-category predicate.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckEntityUtils`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/CheckEntityUtils.java) delegates every selected Enumeration/category token to Commons Lang `NumberUtils.isParsable`; the pinned Commons Lang 3.20.0 implementation iterates `String.charAt` and calls `Character.isDigit(char)`. Read-only a12-dmkits revision `27afb555` iterates Kotlin `Char` in `fieldValueDecimalOrNull`, so its JVM and JavaScript implementations already have the same UTF-16-unit boundary, but its canonical prose and maintained matrix do not name the supplementary-plane rejection.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Keep the existing `FieldValueAsNumber` AST, selected-domain checker, `fieldValueDecimalOrNull`, digit normalization, JVM/Node implementation, and differential harness. Clarify the canonical §5 wording to say UTF-16 character digit class and add one supplementary decimal-code-point rejection beside the existing ASCII/non-ASCII BMP witnesses at the smallest observable owner. If valid-model admission rejects that witness earlier, record the gate boundary instead of manufacturing a conversion differential. Change implementation only for an observed platform divergence. Do not add a Unicode framework, second decimal parser, new harness, or code-point-wide normalization.
- **Compatibility:** Treating decimal digits as ASCII-only rejects legal BMP tokens; treating them as Unicode scalar values admits a supplementary token that the kernel rejects. Either changes static source-domain admission, derived scale availability, validation authoring, and computation eligibility.
- **Acceptance:** The kernel's source-owned conversion path and the JVM/Node peer establish UTF-16 `char` iteration, retain the existing Arabic-Indic/fullwidth and lexical controls, and reject one supplementary decimal-code-point token without adding another parser. In a valid authored kernel model the BMP-only supported-character declaration gate rejects that token before the conversion digit predicate, so this separator is source-established at the conversion seam rather than independently observable through a conversion differential.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `434f4ec8585797677d032639c50b491172bbbf54`
- **Disposition:** accepted with an observability qualification — canonical peer prose records the UTF-16-unit boundary and the JVM/Node controls reject a supplementary decimal digit beside the admitted BMP witnesses. The real kernel rejects the same token earlier at its BMP-only supported-character gate, so agreement is effective rejection through different gates rather than a conversion-stage differential.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-08 — semantic-index Number keys compare by normalized numeric value

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clause:** [`08-paths-and-references.md` §4](../spec/08-paths-and-references.md#4-rows-are-addressed-by-semantic-key-never-by-position-)
- **Delta:** Semantic-index key identity is owned by the declared index-field kind. Number index fields normalize both admitted row keys and requested literal or field-valued keys numerically, so stored/requested decimal spellings such as `5` and `5.00` select the same row. Every non-Number index field retains exact internally stored text identity. Empty, formally invalid, and duplicate index keys remain unavailable under the established validation/computation phase policies.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`IndexFieldCache.normalizeValue`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/IndexFieldCache.java) parses and re-renders both inserted and queried keys only when the index field uses `FormatDefinitionZahl`; every other format returns the raw XML token unchanged. The existing generated mandatory/unique checks establish empty, invalid, and duplicate unavailability before lookup. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, [`Document.RowContext.indexedCell`](../../a12-rulekit/interpreter/src/commonMain/kotlin/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/interpreter/eval/Document.kt) builds and queries a `HashMap<String, ...>` from exact placement text without consulting the index field's declared kind, so a Number row stored as `5.00` does not match the literal key `5` even though the kernel normalizes them.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Correct the existing document-owned semantic-index map rather than adding another evaluator or lookup cache. Derive one canonical key through the index field's existing declared-kind/value conversion: Number keys use the peer's established numeric identity, while every non-Number key stays exact stored text. Apply the same key projection when constructing and querying the map, including field-valued keys, while preserving the current index-column invalidity, duplicate, selected-target, no-match, and phase-precedence owners. Add focused dual-kernel/interpreter controls for literal and field-valued Number keys with `5`/`5.00` spellings, plus a String control proving that the same text pair remains distinct. Do not change target-value conversion, introduce a generic raw-value framework, or add a new harness.
- **Compatibility:** The correction can change which row a semantic index selects when a Number key's legal stored spelling differs from the requested spelling but has the same numeric value. It must not merge non-Number tokens, alter invalid-column poisoning/suppression, select one duplicate participant, or change clean no-match and matched-empty behavior.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer agree on literal and field-valued Number normalization plus the exact-String separator; the existing one document-owned semantic-index map remains the sole row-selection owner; canonical a12-dmkits prose records the kind split; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision and per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `3ceaacae847b3e94e94bc9fff2dd96e2bdf238da` (implementation at `51576dac`)
- **Disposition:** accepted — one declaration-directed `indexKey` projection now owns both semantic-index map construction and lookup: Number keys use canonical numeric identity and every other kind retains exact text. Dual-kernel, JVM, and Node controls cover literal, field-valued, both-spelling-direction, and exact-String separators while preserving duplicate, invalidity, no-match, matched-empty, and phase-precedence behavior.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-09 — fixed group-list predicates admit field and group operands

- **Status:** pending
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`02-logic-and-formal-errors.md` §A.4](../spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md#a4-fill-quantifiers-group-scopes-and-the-two-iteration-ranges) and the [`SEMANTICS-MAP.md` §1 summary](../spec/SEMANTICS-MAP.md#3-the-taxonomy)
- **Delta:** Despite their group-list names, fixed `AllGroupsFilled`, `NoGroupFilled`, `AtLeastOneGroupFilled`, `NotAllGroupsFilled`, and `GroupsNotCollectivelyFilled` conditions admit both field and group operands. A checked field contributes filled, empty, or unavailable directly; a group contributes the same three-way classification from admitted content × error × relevance. Direct non-wildcard duplicates and strict group/descendant overlap are rejected; direct duplicate checking skips wildcarded operands, so the same starred group may occur repeatedly. The all/not-all/not-collectively family requires more than one operand and rejects root groups and wildcards. `NoGroupFilled` and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` admit a singleton, including a sole root group, but reject a root group accompanied by another operand; a fixed singleton has the existing scalar field/group-presence semantics.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckKontextListenBedingungImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/bedingung/CheckKontextListenBedingungImpl.java) applies the operator-specific arity, root, and wildcard checks to a common entity list; [`CheckEntityListenUtils`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/CheckEntityListenUtils.java) admits field and group references, skips direct-duplicate checking for each wildcarded reference in an ordinary A12 model, and still rejects strict indirect group/descendant overlap; [`KontextListenBedingungCreator`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/creation/creators/bedingung/KontextListenBedingungCreator.java), [`ExpandService`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/util/ExpandService.java), and [`EntityListConditionName`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parsetree-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parsetree/a12internal/api/types/EntityListConditionName.java) preserve both entity kinds and authored occurrences through lowering. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, [`GroupListPredicate`](../../a12-rulekit/rulekit/src/main/java/io/github/mbackschat/a12/dm/rulekit/dsl/GroupListPredicate.java) stores only `GroupRef`, so the peer cannot represent the mixed or field-only fixed forms even though its existing group-state evaluator and IF193 laws already own the group operand classification.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Reconcile only the repeated-wildcard follow-up; do not redo the accepted field/group widening at `39af918c` or the mixed-star correction at `b0f78abc`. Qualify the existing direct-duplicate account so ordinary A12 group-list predicates skip each wildcarded operand just as the shared kernel checker does. Through the existing typed authoring/read and interpreter-law owners, admit `NoGroupFilled(G*, G*)` and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled(G*, G*)`, preserve both authored occurrences through rendering/read-back and evaluation input, and retain rejection of repeated direct non-wildcard operands plus strict group/descendant overlap. Reuse the current operand list, checker-arbitrated admission, renderer, reader, and evaluator; add no second predicate, tally, duplicate gate, or harness. Because repeating this structural witness is truth-idempotent for the two legal operators, do not invent an outcome differential that cannot distinguish the correction; lock admission and occurrence preservation at the structural boundaries that can.
- **Compatibility:** This follow-up removes only an over-restrictive direct-duplicate account for wildcarded group operands. It must not alter already accepted field/group classification, mixed-star tally semantics, fixed-singleton canonicalization, non-wildcard duplicate or strict overlap rejection, operator-specific wildcard gates, computation order/poison, or existing group-only callers.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical a12-dmkits prose qualifies the wildcard duplicate exemption; maintained authoring and read-roundtrip controls admit both repeated-star forms and retain two ordered occurrences; the JVM/Node evaluator receives both occurrences without deduplication; neighboring direct non-wildcard duplicate and strict group/descendant overlap controls remain rejected; all accepted `39af918c` and `b0f78abc` behavior remains green; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revisions:** `39af918cdc33dc6175bdc335c1aebc02a7ddbea1`; later mixed-star correction `b0f78abcc98e621bdf37503970b0d3d4b3218886`
- **Disposition:** accepted, then corrected by reviewed inbound evidence — the typed surface now carries a single field-or-group operand sum while preserving group-only constructors. Runtime dispatch reuses the shared field-presence classifier and existing group product state; the same root change also corrected partial field relevance. Admission, execution, JVM/Node, and read-roundtrip locks cover field-only, mixed, unavailable, overlap, arity, root, wildcard, and singleton cases. The later revision corrects this entry's request to keep every starred group on a distinct route: `NoGroupFilled` and `AtLeastOneGroupFilled` admit a starred group mixed with plain operands, each instantiated row contributing one filled count and zero rows contributing nothing. Its validation locks cover the plain-field witness, starred-row witness, zero-row nonwitness, and both firing polarities. The computation-only ordered poison supplement remains under SG4 and is not imported into the validation account. The pre-publication return-type widening is source-compatible for group-only callers but binary-incompatible. A local source recheck then found that the shared direct-duplicate gate skips wildcarded operands, including this group-list family. The peer operand list already retains repeated values and leaves static gates kernel-arbitrated, but its canonical prose and maintained locks do not establish repeated-star admission or occurrence preservation. That bounded verification/correction is pending; no parallel peer implementation is inferred here.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-10 — computation `Having` prefetches one kept successor before target consumption

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §3](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#3-the-filter-having-the--correlation-and-aggregation) and [`09-computations.md` §3.2](../spec/09-computations.md#32-an-invalidity-clear-poisons)
- **Delta:** A computation-phase filtered entity iterator retains first reached filter poison and prefetches one kept successor before the consumer reads the current kept target. After the current candidate is kept, successor search evaluates and skips later false candidates until another candidate is kept or the stream is exhausted. A filter poison during that search therefore wins over a filled current `FirstFilledValue` target. Once one successor has been kept, a terminal current target leaves every filter after that successor unread. An earlier terminal authored operand still hides a later filtered operand completely. Validation keeps its established known-true selector and unknown-as-drop projection.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe` obtains target identifiers through `EntityIterator`; `EbenenIterator.InnerIterator.next` constructs the current `IterationState`, immediately advances through `toNext`, and only then returns the current state; `toNext` applies `FilterPredicate.isRelevant` at each complete candidate. `CalculationCache.convertInvalidFieldsForCalculation` moves invalid cells into the computation-invalid set, and its value reads throw when such a filter operand is reached. This yields exactly one kept-candidate lookahead rather than eager whole-selection filtering. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, `ExprEval.firstFilledScan` evaluates a row's filter and then immediately consumes that row's target, so it under-reads the successor filter and has no maintained separator for this iterator boundary.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Correct the existing shared filtered `FirstFilledValue` traversal rather than adding a second filter AST, target evaluator, scheduler, or iterator framework. Preserve authored operand order, existing filter/cascade observation, target-cell prefix termination, empty/fillability behavior, and both kernel strategies. Add focused dual-kernel/interpreter controls where (1) the first filter is true and the immediate successor filter is invalid, so a filled first target poisons; (2) the first and second filters are true, a third filter is invalid, and the filled first target succeeds because the third filter is unread; (3) a false second row followed by an invalid third filter poisons while searching for the successor; and (4) an earlier direct terminal operand leaves a later filtered operand wholly unread. Reuse existing differential and multiplatform facilities; do not add a capture route or generic scheduling mechanism.
- **Compatibility:** The current peer can return a filled current target before observing a kernel-reached successor-filter failure. Correcting the traversal changes only filtered computation read order and resulting poison/value outcomes. It must not make filtering globally eager, expose filters after one prefetched successor when the current target terminates, alter validation's false/unknown row drop, or weaken operand-level suffix invisibility.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer agree on all four read-order separators; the existing shared filter and `FirstFilledValue` owners remain sole mechanisms; validation and unfiltered controls remain green; canonical a12-dmkits prose names the one-kept-successor boundary; and the handback supplies the committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `f4642dfec13fcf79d37467a0d63710486d511c0d`
- **Disposition:** accepted — one shared kept-successor traversal now performs filter-before-target lookahead while `FirstFilledValue` retains its stop-at-first target consumer. Red/green separators cover immediate and false-candidate successor poison, prefetched-successor suffix invisibility, and earlier-operand termination; unfiltered and validation behavior remain unchanged.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-11 — filtered computation value aggregates share kept-successor lookahead

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §3](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#3-the-filter-having-the--correlation-and-aggregation) and [`09-computations.md` §3.2](../spec/09-computations.md#32-an-invalidity-clear-poisons)
- **Delta:** Computation-phase filtered `Sum`, `MinValue`, `MaxValue`, and `NumberOfDifferentValues` use the same entity iterator and one-kept-successor lookahead as every filtered field-list consumer. They still accumulate every clean kept target rather than stopping at the first value. Internally, a filter poison found while locating the next kept candidate precedes the pending current target; after one successor is kept, a poisoned current target aborts before any filter beyond that successor. False candidates remain part of successor search. An unavailable earlier authored entity-list slot terminates before a later filtered slot is resolved. Validation's unknown-as-drop filter projection and aggregate fillability remain unchanged. The kernel's public computation outcome is cause-blind for these conflicting reached failures, so the filter-versus-target source distinction is not independently observable when a draining aggregate reaches both failures.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: `RuntimeController.combineFeldListe` supplies all four value aggregates from `EntityIterator`; `EbenenIterator.InnerIterator.next` locates one kept successor before returning the current identity; the selected value provider runs only afterward; and `CalculationCache` throws when a reached computation-invalid filter or target cell is read. `NumberCombiner` continues accumulating every clean selected value, so the lookahead changes only filter/target failure order and not successful fold membership. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, `ExprEval.starOperandVals` calls `filter` over every row and only then `mapNotNull` over selected targets, making the complete filter set eager and allowing a later filter failure to win after the kernel would already have stopped on an earlier target failure.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Correct the existing filtered aggregate operand traversal at its shared owner, ideally together with [`SPEC-2026-07-23-10`](#spec-2026-07-23-10--computation-having-prefetches-one-kept-successor-before-target-consumption) if one source-grounded iterator can serve both without changing their distinct target consumers. Keep the established aggregate folds and `FirstFilledValue` prefix evaluator separate. Exercise the conflicting filter/target arrangements, earlier-operand hiding, and clean full accumulation through the maintained dynamic-Groovy/generated-Java and JVM/Node routes, but classify whether the failure source is publicly observable before treating those cases as differentials. Pin an otherwise cause-blind internal order only at its actual traversal owner, with `FirstFilledValue` retaining the public prefix discriminator. Retain neighboring unfiltered and validation controls. Do not add a scheduler, second aggregate evaluator, second filter AST, capture route, or generic runtime framework.
- **Compatibility:** The current peer eagerly reads every aggregate filter before selected targets, disagreeing with the kernel's source-owned traversal even though the public computation result discards which reached failure supplied the poison. Correcting this order preserves current draining-aggregate outcomes while preventing a latent mismatch if a future internal consumer retains richer causes. It must not make aggregation stop after its first clean value, change validation row dropping, alter fold order/precision/identity/fillability, or read later authored operands after a terminal earlier one.
- **Acceptance:** Source inspection confirms the shared one-kept-successor iterator and the kernel's cause-blind computation-result projection. The JVM/Node peer uses the same corrected traversal as `FirstFilledValue`; aggregate agreement locks cover the plausible wrong eager or target-first traversals without claiming that filter-versus-target cause is a public differential. `Sum`, `MinValue`, `MaxValue`, and `NumberOfDifferentValues` retain one shared successful aggregate mechanism, filtered `FirstFilledValue` retains its distinct prefix consumer, and no duplicate traversal/evaluator or new infrastructure is introduced.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `f4642dfec13fcf79d37467a0d63710486d511c0d`
- **Disposition:** accepted with contrary-evidence correction — the eager peer traversal was a real source-level mismatch and now uses the shared kept-successor owner, but the requested aggregate cause-order separator was overdrawn. Both kernel code-generation strategies erase `CalculationFieldException` source identity, and draining aggregates reach the same total failure set; only the `FirstFilledValue` prefix consumer makes this read-order boundary publicly outcome-observable.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-12 — filtered value counts shrink only from a currently selected match

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `434f4ec8585797677d032639c50b491172bbbf54`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`04-numbers-and-decimals.md` §4](../spec/04-numbers-and-decimals.md#4-other-numeric-constraints) and [`03-empty-and-required.md` §2](../spec/03-empty-and-required.md#2-operator-exceptions--where-empty-does-not-just-mean-unknown)
- **Delta:** Complete the `NumberOfValueInFields` count and polarity contract. It counts only filled selected fields equal to the typed constant, so an empty Number never matches zero. Number uses scale-19 equality; textual families use their checked exact projection. Empty cells, omitted tails, and a reached filter permit growth. Unlike ordinary filtered value aggregates, a filter permits shrinkage only when a currently counted matching cell came through that filter. A filtered selected non-match therefore leaves a grow-only zero rather than a both-directionally fillable result. The first reached formal unavailability suppresses the count.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckAnzahlWertOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckAnzahlWertOpImpl.java) owns constant/type/domain admission and fixed scale 0; [`CheckEntityListenUtils`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/util/CheckEntityListenUtils.java) owns multiplicity, duplicate/overlap, value-validation, Enumeration-domain, and shared Boolean-label gates. [`RuntimeController.anzahlWertInFeldlisteIntern`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) distinguishes filter presence from `containsFilteredValue`: every filter makes the result growable, but only a matching filtered value makes it shrinkable. It skips empty values, returns nonrelevance at the first unavailable selected cell, and compares Number through `VkBigDecimal.equals`. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, `NumberOfValueInFieldsDiffTest` locks numeric filled/empty/zero behavior, while the interpreter count route has no retained filter-sensitive polarity separator.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Keep the existing AST, typed field expansion, interpreter count fold, and maintained differential route. Audit the complete Number/String/Enumeration/Boolean/Confirm authoring matrix and preserve the existing exact domain comparisons. Correct only an observed runtime or metadata divergence. Add focused dual-kernel-route plus JVM/Node controls that distinguish a filtered current match from a filtered selected non-match, alongside empty-number-versus-zero, omitted-tail, scale-normalized Number equality, exact token identity, and first-formal-cause controls. Reuse the existing filter traversal and numeric result metadata; do not add a second aggregate evaluator, result type, protocol, or harness.
- **Compatibility:** Treating any reached filter as both-directional overstates omission repair for a selected non-match; treating every filter as grow-only understates the possibility that a currently counted selected match disappears. Empty-to-zero substitution inflates the count, and exact rational equality disagrees with the kernel's scale-19 Number boundary.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer agree on the two filter-polarity separators, empty/zero, omitted-tail, typed equality, and first-unavailability behavior; the complete legal authoring matrix is documented and locked or explicitly corrected at its current owners; no duplicate count/filter mechanism is introduced; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `f4e420856ee0203048a88a5798fb5004afec44ff`
- **Disposition:** accepted after corrective follow-up — the typed count, match-sensitive polarity, empty/zero, omitted-tail, equality, token, and first-unavailability results agree across both kernel strategies and JVM/Node. `NumberOfValueInFields` now consumes the sole `keptRowsWithSuccessorLookahead` filtered traversal owner rather than calling the filter loop directly; the same root correction removes the identical bypass from `NumberOfFilledFields` while preserving each consumer's distinct fold. The focused families, neighboring traversal controls, both interpreter hosts, and full differential estate pass. The interpreter purity lint now rejects a second direct filter-loop call site, so the no-duplicate-mechanism acceptance condition is satisfied without a new iterator, evaluator, result type, harness, protocol, or ledger row.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-13 — over-repetition is an address-formal gate before scalar checking

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `27afb555aae29d3acd4ed04e3aea4772ae85505a`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`01-data-model.md` §2.2](../spec/01-data-model.md#22-over-repetition-and-phantom-rows), [`02-logic-and-formal-errors.md` §B.3](../spec/02-logic-and-formal-errors.md#b3-what-puts-a-cell-in-the-third-state), and [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §5](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#5-groupfilled-and-the-other-repetition-rules)
- **Delta:** Over-repetition is not a generated later annotation over an already scalar-checked cell. Address-formal checking walks the complete repeatable ancestry before scalar conversion; the first coordinate beyond its declared `repeatability` supplies `zuGrosseZeile` or `zuGrosseKontextnummer` as the VALUE formal result, suppresses scalar/custom and ordinary row checking, and makes every descendant read unavailable even when the concrete field is absent. The immutable physical row remains instantiated and therefore still supplies structural group content and error. An in-cap sibling remains ordinary, and an in-cap inner coordinate beneath an over-limit outer ancestor is still over-repetition.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CheckCommand.formalePruefung`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/CheckCommand.java) invokes [`FormalChecker.checkIndices`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/FormalChecker.java) before value conversion and inserts the cell/group into `ValidationCache` only when the address gate succeeds. `checkIndicesLessOrEqualThanMax` walks the complete group ancestry, reports the first excess coordinate, and returns false; immutable `DocumentV2` topology remains unchanged. This is distinct from index-field mandatory and uniqueness rules created by `AutogeneratedRulesService` and run in the later preliminary-rule phase.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Keep one document/address-owned repeatability decision shared by every reader. Confirm that over-limit valid, malformed, absent, and registered-custom descendants all expose over-repetition rather than a scalar/custom result; lock an in-cap sibling and a nested outer-over-limit case; and retain the over-limit row as structural group content/error. Correct canonical prose and runtime only for an observed divergence. Do not add a generated over-repetition rule, second checked-cell model, duplicate ancestry walker, new harness, or mutable-document cleanup.
- **Compatibility:** Running scalar/custom checking first can expose the wrong formal cause, invoke a callback the kernel suppresses, or let an absent descendant read as clean empty. Dropping the physical over-limit row loses group content, while checking only the terminal axis misses an over-limit ancestor.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer agree on valid/malformed/absent/custom descendants, in-cap and nested-ancestor separators, exact phase unavailability, and preserved structural row content/error; one shared address-owned repeatability mechanism remains; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `db55939573bfce92d5fa04e55e9e4cad0b3404bf`
- **Disposition:** accepted — the peer's full-ancestry address gate already preceded scalar/custom checking and suppressed callbacks and descendant reads. One document-owned `overLimitRows` projection now also excludes those physical rows from starred aggregate value domains while preserving them as `GroupFilled`-visible structural content with over-repetition errors. Valid, malformed, absent, custom, in-cap, nested-ancestor, Sum, and filled-count separators are locked.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-14 — partial validation suppresses rather than injects empty index defaults

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `9c4bd66ee894ca3ee96904ab3a57c16c5c94fb3f`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`01-data-model.md` §1](../spec/01-data-model.md#1-model--a-typed-tree), [`10-validation-and-polarity.md` §5](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md#5-full-vs-partial-validation)
- **Delta:** A clean-empty index field eligible for a model-declared stored default has different validation staging by call kind. The default is an ordinary closed Enumeration's S-value stored token; it applies only when the index is not explicitly mandatory, exactly one index-group row exists under the current parent, and that row's index is physically absent or physically present-empty rather than carrying an admitted explicit value. Full validation inserts the default into its transient evaluation cache before preliminary index mandatory/uniqueness and authored-rule evaluation without mutating the document. Partial validation does not insert it and does not emit `mandatoryField` or `uniqueIndex`; it marks the eligible empty index cell unavailable for that call, so generated checks and dependent authored conditions cannot produce errors absent from full validation. Multiple rows, an admitted explicit value, an explicitly mandatory index, and non-Enumeration index kinds receive no default; a formally invalid explicit value remains unavailable and never exposes the S-value to authored evaluation. This is silent call-local suppression, not a generated finding or a phantom physical row.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: A12 model documentation and [`IGroup.getIndexField`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-model-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/model/api/IGroup.java) establish at most one index field per A12 group. [`CheckCommand.formalePruefung`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/CheckCommand.java) omits both absent and present-empty values from the evaluation cache. [`IndexFieldDefaultValueUtils.getIndexFieldIdsEligibleForDefaultValueHandling`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/util/IndexFieldDefaultValueUtils.java) limits defaults to nonmandatory Enumeration S-values, one instantiated group row per parent, and an empty index cache. [`CheckCommand.updateDatenCacheUndUSBCacheAndDamageTmpCache`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/CheckCommand.java) invokes [`ValidationCache.addDefaultValuesForIndexFieldsIfNecessary`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/ValidationCache.java) after formal ingestion. That method inserts the S-value only when the external relevant set is empty; in partial validation it instead marks each eligible empty index instance invalid, propagates error state to its containing groups, and does so before the generated partial preliminary checker and ordinary partial rules run. [`ValidationCommand.processTVInternal`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/ValidationCommand.java) and the generated [`CodeGenRules` template](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-codegen-rules/src/main/resources/internal/templates/validation/java/rulesDir/CodeGenRules.st) establish the later relevant-error-field dispatch.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** First determine whether the current model, adapter, and interpreter represent index-field stored defaults at all. If they do, reuse the existing model-owned default, formal-status cache, partial relevance, generated mandatory/uniqueness, and dependent-read mechanisms to reproduce the full-injected versus partial-silently-unavailable split. If they do not, record the precise model/API gap and its natural owner rather than inventing an interpreter-only default. Lock absent and present-empty defaulted indexes under full and partial validation, an admitted explicit index control, duplicate/default interaction, dependent authored-rule suppression, containing-group error propagation, and no phantom-row creation through both kernel strategies plus JVM/Node when the representation exists. Do not add a second document, preliminary evaluator, partial cache, or harness.
- **Compatibility:** Injecting the default during partial validation can make a dependent rule fire or create duplicate behavior that the kernel suppresses. Treating the clean-empty cell as ordinary empty can emit `mandatoryField`. Emitting a formal message for the call-local invalid marker exposes a channel the kernel keeps silent, while failing to propagate its group error or instantiating a row changes group-presence behavior and later addressing.
- **Acceptance:** The peer either implements the split through its existing model/default and partial-validation owners with both kernel strategies and JVM/Node controls, or reports a bounded model/API gap with a concrete upstream plan. In the implemented case, full validation observes the S-value for exactly one nonmandatory absent or present-empty Enumeration-index row, partial emits neither index generated message, dependent authored conditions are suppressed, the containing group retains error state, admitted explicit values and multiple-row/mandatory/non-Enumeration controls retain ordinary behavior, no phantom row appears, and no duplicate mechanism or harness is added. The handback supplies the exact committed revision and per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `0904542e70b7f0adbb3de4cbab37323059f90ec3`
- **Disposition:** accepted — the model, CLI/schema, and interpreter now carry the declaration-owned Enumeration S-value without an interpreter-only shadow. Full validation stages it transiently without mutating the document; partial validation suppresses the eligible empty index silently while retaining containing-group error state. Kernel, JVM/Node, authoring, and roundtrip controls cover absent/present-empty, explicit, mandatory, multiple-row, dependent-rule, generated-message, and no-phantom-row boundaries.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-15 — computation selects operand indices and shares full default staging

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `39af918cdc33dc6175bdc335c1aebc02a7ddbea1`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`09-computations.md` §3.3](../spec/09-computations.md#33-what-compute-reports)
- **Delta:** In an expanded A12 model, computation's eager formal-input inventory is the union across all checked computations of directly referenced fields, every field contained by ordinary or conditionally referenced groups, and index fields required by semantic-index or parallel-iteration groups; traversal includes operations, common and alternative preconditions, filters, and semantic-index key expressions, after which computed fields are excluded from formal input checking. Formal ingestion then applies the same full-call index S-value staging as full validation. Preliminary mandatory/uniqueness rules run only for index fields retained by that inventory, so an unused bad index is silent, a selected empty or duplicate index reports through `formalErrorsInOperands`, and an eligible selected absent or present-empty Enumeration index receives its declaration-owned default before those rules. This eager inventory is neither a runtime read trace nor a dependency schedule. The legacy unique-entity/H-model path remains outside this expanded-A12-model contract.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`CalculationOperandsCollector`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-calc/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/calc/internal/util/CalculationOperandsCollector.java) traverses every calculation part and categorizes direct field, ordinary group, conditional group, semantic-index, parallel-iteration, filter, and key-expression references. [`CalculationDependencies`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-calc/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/calc/internal/util/CalculationDependencies.java) expands group operands to their contained fields and adds the index fields of referenced index groups to one generated operand set. [`CalculationCommand`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/CalculationCommand.java) retains non-computed members and ancestors, runs full formal ingestion, invokes the inherited full-call default staging, then dispatches preliminary rules only for retained index-field names before converting invalid fields for computation. [`MainValidatorController.preliminaryError`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/MainValidatorController.java) records the formal error and invalidates the addressed index. [`DocumentComputationResultImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-md/kernel-md-runtime-service/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/md/rt/a12internal/service/DocumentComputationResultImpl.java) classifies unmatched messages as formal operand errors rather than computed-instance outcomes. Maintained a12-dmkits `ComputationEagerPrepassLawsTest` at the reviewed basis already locks selected empty/duplicate index errors, ordinary required-empty silence, continued unrelated computation, and the result channel, but does not separate selected from unused indices or default participation.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Extend the existing eager-prepass laws and interpreter/model account at their current owners. Lock a selected versus otherwise identical unused empty/duplicate index, direct index use versus semantic-index/parallel-iteration-induced use, default-eligible absent and present-empty selected indexes, an ineligible explicit/mandatory/multiple-row control, and an unrelated computation that continues. Preserve `formalErrorsInOperands` as the eager result channel, the report-all interpreter's documented projection, and later reached-read poison separately. Reuse the current checked model reference traversal, index/default owner, generated preliminary evaluator, result-channel oracle, and JVM/Node path; do not add a scheduler, second operand collector, second document/prepass, or harness. If the current public model cannot author an index S-value, report that exact model/API gap rather than inventing an interpreter-only default.
- **Compatibility:** Validating every model index reports unrelated errors; using only direct field references misses index fields induced by group expansion, semantic indices, parallel iteration, filters, or key expressions. Skipping full default staging produces a false mandatory error, while treating the eager set as a schedule or global poison can suppress computations that the kernel still evaluates. Folding these errors into generated validation findings or computed-instance errors changes the public V2 result.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer distinguish selected from unused bad indexes and direct from induced index use; eligible defaults participate before selected preliminary rules when the public model can express them; ordinary invalid/required-empty controls and unrelated continued computation remain green; eager formal errors, dependency poison, and generated validation findings remain separate; no duplicate collector, evaluator, scheduler, or harness is introduced; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `0904542e70b7f0adbb3de4cbab37323059f90ec3`
- **Disposition:** accepted — the existing eager computation inventory now distinguishes selected from unused index fields across direct, group-expanded, semantic-index, parallel-iteration, filter, and key-expression references, then shares full-call S-value staging before preliminary index rules. Selected empty/duplicate errors remain in `formalErrorsInOperands`, dependency poison and generated findings remain separate, and unrelated computations continue. The kernel-confirmed target-scope parallel-iteration selection corrected one audit prediction.

### SPEC-2026-07-23-16 — value-list quantifiers preserve operator-specific encounter order

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** introducing commit
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `5350fa17f8bd9721d75039d07a88ab90f5e68d7e`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`06-strings-and-enumerations.md` §B.3](../spec/06-strings-and-enumerations.md#b3-the-value-list-quantifiers--per-cell-three-way-classification-) and [`10-validation-and-polarity.md` §1](../spec/10-validation-and-polarity.md#1-the-core-rule)
- **Delta:** Multi-operand value-list quantifiers do not flatten each side into one global UNKNOWN/filter summary. `AtLeastOne` and `No` consume the complete values side before fields; `NotAll` first performs a fields-presence prepass, consumes values only when a present field exists, then restarts fields. A fields-side `No` match terminates before every later UNKNOWN/filter/tail, while UNKNOWN before the match suppresses. Fields-side `AtLeastOne`/`NotAll` polarity follows the deciding match/outside operand, not an earlier filtered nonwitness. Values-side `AtLeastOne` retains filter polarity only when that filtered operand selected a present member; `No`/`NotAll` retain reached values-side filter, empty, and tail omission potential.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: `RuntimeController.fuegePruefungsrelevanteFeldWerteZuWerteListe` and `mindestensEinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern` own the `AtLeastOne` values-first/witness traversal; `fuegeFeldWerteZuWerteListe` plus `keinFeldWertInWerteListeIntern` own poison-sensitive values materialization and the ordered `No` fields scan; `noEntityFilled` plus `nichtAlleFeldWerteInWerteListeIntern` own the `NotAll` presence-prepass/value/restarted-fields sequence. At the reviewed a12-dmkits basis, `ValueListQuantEval.kt` collapses each side to global `anyUnknown`/`hasHaving` facts, and the maintained `ValueListQuantifierHavingDiffTest` covers filtered deciding operands but not an earlier filtered nonwitness before a later unfiltered witness.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Correct the existing shared value-list evaluator at its traversal root; do not add a second quantifier AST, side model, filter evaluator, or harness. Preserve type admission, category projection, scale-19 Number equality, partial-star extent/nonrelevance, and existing single-operand results. Lock through both kernel strategies plus JVM/Node: (1) fields-side `No` match before later UNKNOWN versus the reverse order, qualifying any public collapse of their terminal no-message results; (2) an earlier filtered nonmatch followed by an unfiltered `AtLeastOne` match and `NotAll` outside witness; (3) a filtered matching/outside fields operand; (4) filtered-empty versus filtered-present values operands for `AtLeastOne`; (5) reached filtered absence for `No`; and (6) `NotAll` with no present fields and an UNKNOWN values member.
- **Compatibility:** The current global summary can report UNKNOWN after a kernel-decisive earlier `No` match and OMISSION when only a filtered nonwitness was encountered. Correcting it changes only multi-operand encounter/filter cases.
