It must not make values lazy for `No`, poison `NotAll` before its presence prepass, erase partial extent, or turn structural address failure into semantic UNKNOWN.
- **Acceptance:** Public kernel outcomes and the JVM/Node peer agree on every message-observable separator; the kernel traversal source and peer evaluator seam additionally retain the internal decided-versus-UNKNOWN order for the `No` separator whose false and unknown results both emit no rule message. Existing scalar and single-star value-list suites remain green; canonical a12-dmkits prose describes the three pass structures and witness-specific filter polarity; one shared evaluator remains; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `7a723254e113db8ac5a774a0644dcd3d69aa2df2`
- **Disposition:** accepted with an observability qualification — one shared evaluator now implements the operator-specific passes and all six separator families are locked across both kernel routes and JVM/Node. For the `No` match-before-UNKNOWN order, the public rule/message projection merges internal FALSE and UNKNOWN into no emitted finding and the DSL exposes no negation combinator; the distinction is therefore source-grounded and pinned at the peer evaluator seam rather than claimed as a message-level differential.

### SPEC-2026-07-24-01 — ordinary rule iteration is derived from condition references

- **Status:** accepted
- **Local revision:** `a44a10cc76d388f8a3e10e12ca802c1f160ace04`
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `f4e420856ee0203048a88a5798fb5004afec44ff`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §1 and checklist](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#1-when-a-rule-iterates-and-where-its-error-lands) and [`SEMANTICS-MAP.md` §3/glossary](../spec/SEMANTICS-MAP.md#3-the-taxonomy)
- **Delta:** Correct the internally contradictory account that the error field helps select ordinary rule iteration. The checked condition's iteration-bearing references derive the scope: ordinary non-starred references contribute their repeatable path, compatible nested references retain the deepest path, and starred/filter-local traversal remains operand-local from the first reopened level downward. The error entity must be referenced directly or indirectly and must inhabit a compatible scope, but it does not introduce an iteration level by declaration placement alone. An iterating scope evaluates only actual rows, preserving complete parent coordinates and document order; zero actual rows produce zero evaluations, while a condition with no iteration-bearing repeatable reference evaluates once.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: `RuleIterationLimitCreator` asks `AnalyseService.isIteration` per level and otherwise emits the once-only bound; `IterationChecker` separately checks error-entity reference membership and iteration consistency; `EbenenIterator` retains complete coordinate vectors. The existing a12-dmkits IF103/IF106 and star-only controls triangulate reference-driven ordinary iteration and operand-local wildcard traversal, while the peer's canonical prose still attributes scope to condition plus error-field references.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Correct the canonical/public §9 account at its existing owners and lock the static admission and runtime-range mechanism through existing model/law facilities. Distinguish: an ordinary repeatable condition reference with a matching error field; an error field deeper than the condition that must not create a deeper loop; compatible nested references selecting the deepest path; zero actual rows versus one instantiated-empty row; a starred aggregate that does not turn its reopened level into surrounding per-row iteration; and an incompatible sibling scope routed to the existing parallel/static rejection boundary. Reuse the current checked rule analysis and runtime paths; do not add another iterator, rule-environment model, evaluator, or harness.
- **Compatibility:** Treating the error declaration as an iteration source can create phantom per-row evaluations, select the wrong nested address, or silently turn an invalid cross-cardinality rule into a different query. Treating a star as surrounding iteration repeats a whole aggregate per member and changes both messages and cost. Synthesizing a row at zero cardinality emits a message where the kernel runs no rule instance, while collapsing nested addresses to the terminal coordinate aliases equal inner row numbers under different parents.
- **Acceptance:** Both kernel strategies and the JVM/Node peer retain the existing observable rule outcomes for the separating cases; checked admission derives ordinary scope from condition references, validates error reference/scope separately, iterates only actual rows in full nested document order, and leaves wildcard/filter iteration with its existing operand owner. The handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition and introduces no duplicate iterator, environment, evaluator, or harness.
- **a12-dmkits revision:** `f43a7ccf68d374a804b7447ee8d02e160abd0d49`
- **Disposition:** accepted — verification-first tri-engine probing found the interpreter already derived ordinary iteration solely from condition references through its existing `IterationScopes` owner. The peer canonical account was corrected and new tri-engine plus JVM/Node locks cover deepest compatible scope, complete parent coordinates, per-parent outer resolution, actual-row document order, zero rows, and once-only noniterating evaluation; the existing owners retain the deeper error-field, star-local, and incompatible-sibling separators. The by-catch additionally confirms the already-canonical `MVK_NEG_CONDITION_IN_ITERATION` boundary: an unguarded pure-negative iterating condition is rejected at the offending level, while a guarded condition authors and evaluates per actual row. No runtime mechanism, iterator, environment, evaluator, or harness was added.

### SPEC-2026-07-24-02 — ordinary-iteration negative admission is level- and operand-sensitive

- **Status:** accepted — reviewed a12-dmkits revision `10451733`, **explicitly partial**; the unlocked remainder is inventoried in the disposition rather than implied
- **Local revision:** `27aee4182d51985c6b454231e229274351da33f7`
- **a12-dmkits basis revision:** `98d2aacc256ef8dc7b9300b273748f8bf6151ed1`
- **Kernel behavior:** 30.8.1
- **Canonical clauses:** [`07-repetition-and-iteration.md` §1](../spec/07-repetition-and-iteration.md#1-when-a-rule-iterates-and-where-its-error-lands) and the [`SEMANTICS-MAP.md` §9 summary](../spec/SEMANTICS-MAP.md#3-the-taxonomy)
- **Delta:** Negative-condition admission is checked independently at every ordinary iteration level. `And` needs one child that both references and is admitted at that level; `Or` requires every child to reference and be admitted. Leaf admission depends on operator and operands. For a direct iterating Number field compared with an immediate number literal, ordinary `==`, `>=`, and `<=` are unguarded exactly when the kernel's host-converted integer form of the literal is zero; strict comparisons, ordinary `!=`, tolerance-not-equal, and a converted nonzero literal are admitted, symmetrically in both operand orders. The conversion parses the finite decimal to nearest-even binary64, applies Java `Math.round` with long saturation, and narrows to signed 32-bit by two's-complement wrap before testing zero or positivity. Exact rational value plus authored fractional-digit count determines the conversion because the grammar has no exponent and parsing removes only redundant leading zeros; signed zero is conversion-equivalent. A below-half binary64 tie and positive `2^32` are concrete separators from exact rational classification. Direct evaluated-String `Length(field)`, direct `RangeAsNumber(field, start, end)`, direct `FieldValueAsNumber(field)`, and direct numeric Day/Month/Quarter/Year or Hour/Minute/Second extraction from a temporal field are also zero-sensitive field operations. Each target supplies ordinary scope; addressed `Length` reads the exact checked String through the UTF-16 evaluator, addressed range selection retains its checked interval and missing-versus-present-fallback polarity, addressed conversion retains the model-certified String or stored/category Enumeration projection and domain-derived scale, and addressed temporal extraction retains its Date/Time/DateTime kind and component-availability certificate before reusing the existing decoded component evaluator. Empty temporal input remains symmetric fillable zero, formal invalidity remains UNKNOWN, and structural addressing failure remains outside semantic UNKNOWN. Fixed Base-Year extraction contributes no field scope. Date-only `DifferenceInMonths` and `DifferenceInYears` remain distinct two-operand zero-sensitive operations: each field operand contributes its declaration-owned scope, compatible prefixes merge to the deeper scope, and any Base-Year operand contributes none. `DifferenceInDays` shares that scope and zero-admission account but separately admits DateTime and retains the checked concrete model-zone profile, decoded local labels, and already-resolved exact instants. `DifferenceInHours`, `DifferenceInMinutes`, and `DifferenceInSeconds` share the ordered two-field scope and zero-admission branch but admit DateTime fields only, require the selected time component and compatible explicit-year presence, reject Base Year, and retain the selected unit plus each exact instant for signed epoch-millisecond division with truncation toward zero. Addressed difference runtime reads field operands in authored order through one checked document, preserving signed results, symmetric missing zero, formal UNKNOWN, and structural failure outside UNKNOWN. Top-level operation-list `Abs`, rounding, and operand-list Min/Max whose direct Number references all iterate at the queried level retain that zero-sensitive partition. If an operation-list or entity-list reference set mixes iterating and noniterating references at that level, every immediate numeric-literal comparison is unguarded regardless of operator, operand direction, or literal conversion; the zero/positive family partitions apply only to an all-iterating set. A filtered slot contributes its selected target reference, not the references inside its `Having`, to this static operation classifier. For one-scope plain or filtered-star Number entity lists, `FirstFilledValue` and value aggregates are zero-sensitive, distinct count is positive-threshold-sensitive under direction-specific strict/inclusive/not-equal/tolerance comparisons, and value count has both sensitivities. String/Enumeration `NumberOfValueInFields` has the same two sensitivities. Row-aligned plain-star `SumOfProducts` is zero-sensitive without the positive-count branch. A top-level ordinary arithmetic or power expression is a composite operation and bypasses those direct field/list-versus-constant branches, so wrapping a field can change static admission without changing runtime arithmetic. The map's stale shared rule/computation sentence is also corrected: only condition references select ordinary rule scope; the error entity does not.
- **Basis:** Kernel revision `cb66e51fa7ab90b650698f861bf670754e2e1e66`: [`IterationNotValidVisitor`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/util/IterationNotValidVisitor.java) enumerates relevant levels in order, applies the connective rules, and dispatches the direct comparison zero family, including direct field operations. [`FeldOpCreator`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/creation/creators/operation/FeldOpCreator.java) retains `Length` and `FieldValueAsNumber` as distinct direct `IFieldIdOperation` shapes, while [`CheckFeldOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckFeldOpImpl.java) owns the latter's complete String/Enumeration/category domain certificate. [`FeldOpMitParamsCreator`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/creation/creators/operation/FeldOpMitParamsCreator.java) retains `RangeAsNumber` as the parameterized subtype of that same field-operation interface, and [`CheckFeldOpMitParamsImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/operation/CheckFeldOpMitParamsImpl.java) owns its String-like source and interval checks. Runtime [`RuntimeController.feldWertAlsZahl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) and [`RuntimeController.bereichAlsZahl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-rt/kernel-core-runtime/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/rt/_30_8/internal/core/RuntimeController.java) construct or receive the row-specific identifier before applying their respective conversions. [`FeldOperationUtil`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parsetree-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parsetree/a12internal/services/FeldOperationUtil.java) owns the `Double.parseDouble` → `Math.round` → `(int)` conversion used for the comparison literal. [`KonstanteCreator`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/creation/creators/operation/KonstanteCreator.java) retains a finite decimal without exponent after removing only redundant leading zeros, which makes exact rational value plus fractional-digit count a sufficient clean-room carrier. [`ZusGesetzteFeldOperationCreator`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/creation/creators/operation/ZusGesetzteFeldOperationCreator.java) constructs arithmetic and power as `ICompositeOperation`, for which the visitor has no corresponding direct-operand branch. The reviewed a12-dmkits basis documents only the general guard advice, deliberately delegates the full visitor to the kernel, and has no maintained direct-Number admission matrix for this partition.
- **Temporal extraction basis:** The same visitor classifies every [`IDateExtractionOperation`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parsetree-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parsetree/a12internal/api/operation/date/IDateExtractionOperation.java) as zero-sensitive. [`DatumExtractOpCreator`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/creation/creators/operation/DatumExtractOpCreator.java) retains the seven numeric names as one operation family; [`CheckDatumExtractOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumExtractOpImpl.java) rejects constants and incompatible kinds/components before assigning NUMBER; the generated runtime call delegates the selected row value to the corresponding `RuntimeController` component method. The two DateTime-returning extraction names are DATE operations and remain outside this numeric atom.
- **Date-difference basis:** The same visitor classifies each [`IDateDiffOperation`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parsetree-api/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parsetree/a12internal/api/operation/date/IDateDiffOperation.java) through its direct zero-sensitive branch. [`CheckDatumDiffOpImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/check/datum/CheckDatumDiffOpImpl.java) requires two completely specified compatible Date operands for month/year differences and assigns NUMBER scale 0; [`DateDiffOperationImpl`](../../a12-kernel/kernel-tool/kernel-core-parser/src/main/java/com/mgmtp/a12/kernel/core/parser/internal/parsebaum/impl/operation/datum/DateDiffOperationImpl.java) retains their authored order. Generated calls preserve that order, and `BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDatumsDiff` gives either missing operand symmetric fillable zero, gives formal invalidity precedence, and otherwise returns the signed completed-period difference.
- **Calendar-day basis:** The same checker admits Date, DateTime, and mixed date-bearing operands with a day component for `DifferenceInDays`, while profile selection remains model-owned. Generated calls preserve operand order. `BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDifferenzInTagen` counts model-zone calendar steps, and the checked Lean atom retains the selected concrete profile plus both decoded local labels and exact instants rather than re-resolving stored overlap labels. Accepted [`SPEC-2026-07-20-08`](#spec-2026-07-20-08--differenceindays-counts-model-zone-calendar-steps) owns the calendar-step behavior itself; this entry adds only its repeatable scope and addressed rule integration.
- **Elapsed-difference basis:** `CheckDatumDiffOpImpl` admits hour/minute/second differences only when both operands are DateTime-compatible date specifications exposing the selected component and compatible explicit-year presence; Base Year, Date, and Time are rejected. `DateDiffOperationImpl` and generated calls retain the two operands in authored order. `IterationNotValidVisitor` assigns the same direct zero-sensitive branch, while `BedingungsOperatorHelper.getDatumsDiff` gives formal unavailability precedence, maps either missing operand to symmetric fillable zero, and otherwise divides the exact signed epoch-millisecond delta by the selected positive unit with truncation toward zero.
- **Requested a12-dmkits reconciliation:** Keep kernel delegation and the existing condition AST, linter, `RuleGroupIterationLawsTest`, and diagnostic owner. Correct the canonical §9 account and extend the smallest existing kernel admission-law family with both operand orders; `==`/`>=`/`<=` zero rejections; strict/`!=`/tolerance zero acceptances; a nonzero integral acceptance; direct evaluated-String `Length`, direct String `RangeAsNumber`, and direct String/stored-or-category Enumeration `FieldValueAsNumber` scope/zero/runtime controls, including the range's missing-versus-present-fallback polarity; direct-field `Abs`/rounding and all-iterating direct Min/Max zero controls; mixed-level direct operation-list and mixed direct/star entity-list any-literal rejections; plain/filtered-star `FirstFilledValue`/Sum/distinct-count/Number and String/Enumeration value-count zero-versus-positive separators in both operand directions, including a filter-only noniterating reference that must not make the operation set mixed; row-aligned plain-star `SumOfProducts` zero and nonzero controls; the direct-field-versus-composite-arithmetic zero separator; and source-grounded below-half tie, negative-half, long-saturation, and signed-32-bit wrap controls where the public DSL preserves the required literal. Do not copy the full visitor into the interpreter or linter, add another condition tree, or create a new harness.
- **Temporal addition to the request:** Extend the same existing admission-law family with direct Date, Time, and DateTime numeric-component sources: both operand orders around converted zero, one admitted nonzero control, incompatible kind/component authoring rejection, empty symmetric-zero polarity, formal invalidity, and exact row-address execution. Add separate `DifferenceInMonths`/`DifferenceInYears` controls for ordered field operands, compatible scope derivation, a Base-Year/field case, zero admission, signed runtime order, missing/formal polarity, and structural address failure. Add `DifferenceInDays` Date/DateTime and Base-Year/field controls that preserve its selected model-zone profile and exact stored instants. Add DateTime-only `DifferenceInHours`/`DifferenceInMinutes`/`DifferenceInSeconds` controls for selected-component and year-presence admission, Base-Year/Date/Time rejection, both operand orders, positive and negative fractional truncation, all three units, missing/formal polarity, exact checked addresses, and structural failure. Keep every two-operand operation distinct from direct extraction, reuse the current temporal evaluator and rule iteration path, and do not add an operation-specific environment or pair stream.
- **Compatibility:** A global negative-operator list, a single whole-tree emptiness bit, exact-decimal zero testing, flattening composite arithmetic to its field references, or flattening temporal extraction to an untyped Number field misclassifies legal and illegal models and may report the wrong repeatable level. Replicating only the common integral-zero examples while claiming complete parity misses host-conversion, parse-tree-topology, and temporal component-certificate edge cases.
- **Acceptance:** Canonical a12-dmkits prose states the per-level connective, direct-Number, direct temporal-component, and ordered calendar/elapsed temporal-difference scope rules, all-iterating versus mixed-reference operation/entity-list, filter-reference exclusion, family-specific entity-list, and `SumOfProducts` partitions plus the composite-operation separator; maintained kernel admission locks cover every public separator above or explicitly document an unrepresentable raw-literal boundary; direct temporal and difference runtime locks preserve kind/component admission, decoded row projection, concrete day profile, exact elapsed instants and units, operand order, missing/formal polarity, and structural addressing; the interpreter remains unchanged unless runtime evaluation diverges independently; no duplicate visitor, evaluator, pair stream, or harness is added; and the handback supplies the exact committed revision plus per-surface disposition.
- **Disposition (reviewed a12-dmkits `10451733`): the canonical correction landed; the evidence half is partial and honestly bounded.** Their `RuleGroupIterationLawsTest` grew from one case to thirteen inside the existing family — no new harness, no ported visitor, kernel delegation unchanged, exactly as requested. Canonical §9 prose now carries the per-level connective rule, the converted-zero partition, and the composite separator, with a new findings supplement holding the measured matrix.
- **Locked upstream:** the direct-Number partition in both operand orders; the conversion boundary (`0.40`/`0.49` reject versus `0.50`/`0.60`/`1.00` admit); the negative-half asymmetry; direct `Abs`, all-iterating operand-list `MinValue`/`MaxValue`, and evaluated-String `Length`; and the direct-versus-composite-arithmetic separator.
- **Named upstream as NOT locked, which is the larger part of this entry's matrix:** `RangeAsNumber`/`FieldValueAsNumber` scope and runtime controls, temporal component extraction, the ordered `DifferenceIn{Months,Years,Days,Hours,Minutes,Seconds}` family, the entity-list and aggregate partitions (`FirstFilledValue`, distinct and value counts, `NumberOfValueInFields`, `SumOfProducts`), mixed-level any-literal rejection, and filter-reference exclusion. Recording that inventory rather than claiming completion is the correct outcome — it is precisely the overclaim this entry's compatibility note warned about, and those clauses stay `Kernel-locked` negative here.
- **One requested half needed no upstream change:** their §9 already derived ordinary scope from condition references and already stated that the error entity is not an anchor. This entry's scope-derivation delta was therefore a local restatement of an account the peer already held, not a correction to it.
- **Two requested controls are unrepresentable through the public DSL:** long saturation and the `2^32` wrap need a literal the authoring scale gate or the grammar rejects before the admission visitor runs. Verified upstream at the *conversion* level instead, which this entry's acceptance condition explicitly permits as a documented unrepresentable boundary.
- **Follow-up originated by the same review:** the accompanying read-only check of this repository's `spec/` proposed dropping the below-half separator as a pre-Java-7 artifact. That is half right, and the correction is now [`SPEC-2026-07-25-02`](#spec-2026-07-25-02--the-below-half-separator-is-real-but-it-is-a-parse-time-mechanism-rather-than-a-mathround-one).
